\displaystyle=\bar{\bar{y}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\overline{\epsilon}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{B}}-\overline{\epsilon}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{S}}+\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{BS}}

Moreover, the residuals are mean zero for each
\gamma
and uncorrelated between different groups:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{S}}]=\mathbb{E}\left[\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}}\right]=0,

\displaystyle\Cov(\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}},\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{S}})

\displaystyle=\Cov(\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}},\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{BS}})=\Cov(\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{S}},\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{BS}})=0.

Proposition A.1

(Generic Variance Formula)
.

\text{Var}_{\gamma}:=\Var(\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\gamma})=\frac{I-I_{\gamma}}{I_{\gamma}I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}+\frac{J-J_{\gamma}}{J_{\gamma}J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma}+\frac{I-I_{\gamma}}{I_{\gamma}I}\frac{J-J_{\gamma}}{J_{\gamma}J}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}

Proof.

By the Hoeffding decomposition above, the variance of
\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\gamma}
is simply
\Var(\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}})+\Var(\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{S}})+\Var(\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}})
, and so we must calculate the variance of each term. By the computation in Lemma A.7 of
[
23
]
, we expand out the square in
\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}}
and cancel terms using the fact that
\sum_{i}^{I}\delta_{i}^{\textsf{B}}(\gamma)=0.
Carrying out the details leads to the conclusion

\displaystyle\Var_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}^{2}}\frac{(I-I_{\gamma})I_{\gamma}}{I^{2}(I-1)}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\delta_{i}^{\textsf{B}}(\gamma)^{2}+\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}^{2}}\frac{(I-I_{\gamma})I_{\gamma}}{I^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\delta_{i}^{\textsf{B}}(\gamma)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}^{2}}\frac{(I-I_{\gamma})I_{\gamma}}{I(I-1)}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\delta_{i}^{\textsf{B}}(\gamma)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{I-I_{\gamma}}{I_{\gamma}I}\omega_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}}.

The quantity
\Var(\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{S}})
gives the analogous result by the same computation:

\Var_{\gamma}^{\textsf{S}}=\frac{J-J_{\gamma}}{J_{\gamma}J}\omega_{\gamma}^{\textsf{S}}.

Finally the doubly decentered term
\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}}
is handled in a similar fashion by expanding out the square.

\displaystyle\Var_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}^{2}J_{\gamma}^{2}}\frac{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}(I-I_{\gamma})(J-J_{\gamma})}{I^{2}J^{2}}\frac{IJ}{(I-1)(J-1)}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\delta_{ij}^{\textsf{BS}}(\gamma)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{I-I_{\gamma}}{I_{\gamma}I}\frac{J-J_{\gamma}}{J_{\gamma}J}\omega_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}}.

∎

Corollary A.1

(Explicit Variance Formulas)
.

\displaystyle\Var_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}+\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}

\displaystyle\Var_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}+\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}

\displaystyle\Var_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}

\displaystyle\Var_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}.

Proposition A.2

(Generic Covariance Formula)
.

Let
\eta_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{B}}\in\{-1,+1\}
be
+1
if groups
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}
share the same rows, and
-1
otherwise. Similarly, let
\eta_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{S}}\in\{-1,+1\}
be
+1
if groups
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}
share the same columns, and
-1
otherwise. Then

\displaystyle\Cov_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}:=\Cov(\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\gamma},\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\gamma^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\eta_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{B}}\frac{I_{C}I_{T}}{I_{\gamma}I_{\gamma^{\prime}}}\frac{1}{2I}\left(\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}-\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\eta_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{S}}\frac{J_{C}J_{T}}{J_{\gamma}J_{\gamma^{\prime}}}\frac{1}{2J}\left(\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}-\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\eta_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{B}}\eta_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{S}}\frac{I_{C}I_{T}J_{C}J_{T}}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}I_{\gamma^{\prime}}J_{\gamma^{\prime}}}\frac{1}{2IJ}\left(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}+\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}-\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\right)

Proof.

By
Lemma

A.1
, it can be seen that

\Cov(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}_{\gamma},\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}_{\gamma^{\prime}})=\eta_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{B}}\Cov(\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}},\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{B}})+\eta_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{S}}\Cov(\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{S}},\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{S}})+\eta_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{B}}\eta_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{S}}\Cov(\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}},\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{BS}}).

The computation in Lemma A.8 of
[
23
]
shows

\displaystyle\Cov(\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}},\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{B}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}I_{\gamma^{\prime}}}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\left(\frac{I_{C}I_{T}}{I^{2}(I-1)}+\frac{I_{C}I_{T}}{I^{2}}\right)\delta_{i}^{\textsf{B}}(\gamma)\delta_{i}^{\textsf{B}}(\gamma^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{I_{C}I_{T}}{I_{\gamma}I_{\gamma^{\prime}}I(I-1)}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\delta_{i}^{\textsf{B}}(\gamma)\delta_{i}^{\textsf{B}}(\gamma^{\prime}).

Next,

\displaystyle\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}-\frac{2}{I-1}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\delta_{i}^{\textsf{B}}(\gamma)\delta_{i}^{\textsf{B}}(\gamma^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}-\frac{2I_{\gamma}I_{\gamma^{\prime}}I}{I_{C}I_{T}}\Cov(\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}},\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{B}}).

Rearranging gives

\Cov(\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}},\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{B}})=\frac{I_{C}I_{T}}{2I_{\gamma}I_{\gamma^{\prime}}I}\left(\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}-\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\right).

An analogous computation gives

\Cov(\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{S}},\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{S}})=\frac{J_{C}J_{T}}{2J_{\gamma}J_{\gamma^{\prime}}J}\left(\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}-\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\right).

For the buyer-seller term, the computation in Lemma A.8 of
[
23
]
gives

\Cov(\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}},\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{BS}})=\frac{I_{C}I_{T}J_{C}J_{T}}{I_{\gamma}I_{\gamma^{\prime}}J_{\gamma}J_{\gamma^{\prime}}}\frac{1}{IJ(I-1)(J-1)}\sum_{i,j}\delta_{ij}^{\textsf{BS}}(\gamma)\delta_{ij}^{\textsf{BS}}(\gamma^{\prime})

Expanding
\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}
and rearranging as before gives

\Cov(\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}},\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{BS}})=\frac{I_{C}I_{T}J_{C}J_{T}}{I_{\gamma}I_{\gamma^{\prime}}J_{\gamma}J_{\gamma^{\prime}}}\frac{1}{2IJ}\left(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}+\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}-\xi_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}^{\textsf{BS}}\right),

as desired.
∎

The following corollary writes out the explicit formulas for the covariances, fixing small typos in an earlier version of
[
23
]
.

Corollary A.2

(Explicit Covariance Formulas; Lemma A.8 of
[
23
]
)
.

\displaystyle\Cov_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{I_{C}}{2I_{T}I}\left(\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}-\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2J}\left(\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}-\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}\right)-\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}}\frac{1}{2IJ}\left(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}+\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}-\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}\right)

\displaystyle\Cov_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2I}\left(\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}\right)+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}}\frac{1}{2J}\left(\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}\right)-\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}}\frac{1}{2IJ}\left(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}+\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}\right)

\displaystyle\Cov_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2I}\left(\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2J}\left(\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)+\frac{1}{2IJ}\left(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}+\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)

\displaystyle\Cov_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2I}\left(\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2J}\left(\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}\right)+\frac{1}{2IJ}\left(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}+\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}\right)

\displaystyle\Cov_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2I}\left(\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}}\frac{1}{2J}\left(\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)-\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}}\frac{1}{2IJ}\left(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}+\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)

\displaystyle\Cov_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}}\frac{1}{2I}\left(\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2J}\left(\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)-\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}}\frac{1}{2IJ}\left(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}+\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)

A.1
Direct Effect

Recalling the direct effect estimator
\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}}-\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}}-\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}}+\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}}
, the population variance of the estimator can be written explicitly using the variance and covariance formulas earlier in this section. The resulting expression is:

\displaystyle\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}+\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}

\displaystyle+\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}+\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}

\displaystyle+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}

\displaystyle+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}

\displaystyle-\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}\left(\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}-\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}\right)+\frac{1}{J}\left(\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}-\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}\right)+\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}}\frac{1}{IJ}\left(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}+\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}-\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{I}\left(\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}\right)-\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}}\frac{1}{J}\left(\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}\right)+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}}\frac{1}{IJ}\left(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}+\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{I}\left(\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)-\frac{1}{J}\left(\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)+\frac{1}{IJ}\left(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}+\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{I}\left(\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}\right)-\frac{1}{J}\left(\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}\right)+\frac{1}{IJ}\left(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}+\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{I}\left(\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)-\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}}\frac{1}{J}\left(\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}}\frac{1}{IJ}\left(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}+\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}}\frac{1}{I}\left(\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)+\frac{1}{J}\left(\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}}\frac{1}{IJ}\left(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}+\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)

A close inspection reveals that all first order terms
\omega_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}},\omega_{\gamma}^{\textsf{S}}
in the expression cancel. Further, grouping all the
\omega_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}}
terms yields
\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}\omega_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}}
for each
\gamma\in\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}\}.
Thus we may simplify the expression above to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I_{T}J_{T}}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}+\frac{1}{I_{T}J_{C}}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}+\frac{1}{I_{C}J_{T}}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}+\frac{1}{I_{C}J_{C}}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}

\displaystyle+\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}-\frac{1}{I}\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}+\frac{1}{I}\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}+\frac{1}{I}\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\frac{1}{I}\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}}\frac{1}{I}\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{J}\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}}\frac{1}{J}\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}+\frac{1}{J}\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}+\frac{1}{J}\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}}\frac{1}{J}\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\frac{1}{J}\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle-\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}}\frac{1}{IJ}\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}-\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}}\frac{1}{IJ}\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\frac{1}{IJ}\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\frac{1}{IJ}\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}}\frac{1}{IJ}\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}}\frac{1}{IJ}\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}.

A.2
Total Effect

From the results of
Appendix

A
, the population variance formula for the total effect estimator is given as follows.

\Var(\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}}-\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}})=\Var(\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}})+\Var(\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}})-2\Cov(\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}},\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}}).

Explicitly,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Var(\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}})&=\frac{I_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}}{I_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}I}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{J_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}}{J_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}J}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{I_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}J_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}}{I_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}IJ_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}J}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{BS}}\\
\Var(\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}})&=\frac{I_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}}{I_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}I}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{J_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}}{J_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}J}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{I_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}J_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}}{I_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}J_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}IJ}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{BS}}\\
\Cov(\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}},\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}})&=-\frac{1}{2I}\left(\omega_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{B}}+\omega_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{B}}-\xi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{B}}\right)\\
&-\frac{1}{2J}\left(\omega_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{S}}+\omega_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{S}}-\xi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{S}}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{2IJ}\left(\omega_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{BS}}+\omega_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{BS}}-\xi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{BS}}\right)\end{split}

(56)

It is helpful to rearrange some terms in the variance formula above. Notice that
\alpha_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{1}{I}=\frac{1}{I_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}},\alpha_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{1}{J}=\frac{1}{J_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}}
and similarly with
\alpha_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{1}{I}=\frac{1}{I_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}},\alpha_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{1}{J}=\frac{1}{J_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}}
. As a result,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Var(\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}}-\widehat{\overline{\overline{Y}}}_{\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}})&=\frac{1}{I_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{1}{J_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{1}{IJ}\left(\frac{I_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}J_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}}{I_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}J_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}}-1\right)\omega_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{BS}}\\
&+\frac{1}{I_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{1}{J_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{1}{IJ}\left(\frac{I_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}J_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}}{I_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}J_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}}-1\right)\omega_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{BS}}\\
&-\frac{1}{I}\xi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{B}}-\frac{1}{J}\xi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{1}{IJ}\xi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{BS}}\end{split}

(57)

A.3
Spillover Effects

For completeness, we also include variance formulas for estimators of the spillover effects. The
buyer spillover
and
seller spillover
effects are defined by
\bar{\bar{y}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}-\bar{\bar{y}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}},\bar{\bar{y}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\bar{\bar{y}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}
respectively. Consider the non-interacted regression adjustment (
7
) for the buyer spillover, which we denote
\tau_{\text{bs}}(\beta)
, and
\tau_{\text{ss}}(\beta)
for the seller spillover. Using the full formulas for the variances and covariances of group means, we have

\displaystyle\Var(\tau_{\text{bs}}(\beta))

\displaystyle=\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}+\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I}\left(\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)-\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}}\frac{1}{J}\left(\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}}\frac{1}{IJ}\left(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}+\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right).

The seller terms cancel, yielding

\displaystyle\Var(\tau_{\text{bs}}(\beta))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{I_{T}}\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}+\frac{1}{I_{C}}\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}+\frac{J_{T}}{I_{T}J_{C}J}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}+\frac{J_{T}}{I_{C}J_{C}J}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I}\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}}\frac{1}{IJ}\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}.

Carrying out the same computation for the seller spillover effects, we obtain

\displaystyle\Var(\tau_{\text{ss}}(\beta))

\displaystyle=\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}}\frac{1}{I}\left(\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)+\frac{1}{J}\left(\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right)+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}}\frac{1}{IJ}\left(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}+\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}\right).

Here, the buyer terms cancel, yielding

\displaystyle\Var(\tau_{\text{ss}}(\beta))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{J_{T}}\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}+\frac{1}{J_{C}}\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}+\frac{I_{T}}{J_{T}I_{C}I}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}+\frac{I_{T}}{J_{C}I_{C}I}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}}\frac{1}{I}\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\frac{1}{J}\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}}\frac{1}{IJ}\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}.

Appendix B
Proofs for
Section

2

Proof of
Example

2.1
.

To make
\tau
identifiable, we enforce the following constraints:

\displaystyle\sum_{i}(1-W_{i}^{\textsf{B}})\mu_{i}^{\textsf{B}}=\sum_{j}(1-W_{j}^{\textsf{S}})\mu_{j}^{\textsf{S}}=0

\displaystyle\sum_{i}(1-W_{i}^{\textsf{B}})\delta_{i}^{\textsf{B}}=\sum_{j}(1-W_{j}^{\textsf{S}})\alpha_{j}^{\textsf{S}}=0

\displaystyle\sum_{i}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}(\mu_{i}^{\textsf{B}}+\alpha_{i}^{\textsf{B}})=\sum_{j}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}(\mu_{j}^{\textsf{S}}+\delta_{j}^{\textsf{S}})=0

\displaystyle\sum_{i}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}(\delta_{i}^{\textsf{B}}+\tau_{i}^{\textsf{B}})=\sum_{j}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}(\alpha_{j}^{\textsf{S}}+\tau_{j}^{\textsf{S}})=0.

Define

\displaystyle\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}

\displaystyle:=\mu+\alpha+\delta+\tau

\displaystyle\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}

\displaystyle:=\mu+\alpha

\displaystyle\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}

\displaystyle:=\mu+\delta

\displaystyle\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle:=\mu

and analogously define
\Delta_{\gamma,i}^{\textsf{B}},\Delta^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma,j}
for the fixed effects. The WLS objective can then be written

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I_{T}^{2}J_{T}^{2}}\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}\left(y_{ij}-X_{ij}^{\top}\beta-\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}-\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},i}^{\textsf{B}}-\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},j}^{\textsf{S}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I_{T}^{2}J_{C}^{2}}\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}(1-W_{j}^{\textsf{S}})\left(y_{ij}-X_{ij}^{\top}\beta-\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}-\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},i}^{\textsf{B}}-\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},j}^{\textsf{S}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I_{C}^{2}J_{T}^{2}}\sum_{i,j}(1-W_{i}^{\textsf{B}})W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}\left(y_{ij}-X_{ij}^{\top}\beta-\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},i}^{\textsf{B}}-\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},j}^{\textsf{S}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I_{C}^{2}J_{C}^{2}}\sum_{i,j}(1-W_{i}^{\textsf{B}})(1-W_{j}^{\textsf{S}})\left(y_{ij}-X_{ij}^{\top}\beta-\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}},i}^{\textsf{B}}-\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}},j}^{\textsf{S}}\right)^{2}.

The identifiability constraints are chosen exactly to force
\sum_{i\in\gamma}\Delta_{\gamma,i}^{\textsf{B}}=\sum_{j\in\gamma}\Delta_{\gamma,j}^{\textsf{S}}=0
.
Recall the well-known fact that regardless of
\beta
,
\Delta_{\gamma},\Delta_{\gamma,i}^{\textsf{B}},\Delta_{\gamma,j}^{\textsf{S}}
doubly decenters each of the matrices. Thus the optimal
\beta
must minimize

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I_{T}^{2}J_{T}^{2}}\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}\left(y_{ij}^{(dcr)}-X_{ij}^{(dcr)\top}\beta\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I_{T}^{2}J_{C}^{2}}\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}(1-W_{j}^{\textsf{S}})\left(y_{ij}^{(dcr)}-X_{ij}^{(dcr)\top}\beta\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I_{C}^{2}J_{T}^{2}}\sum_{i,j}(1-W_{i}^{\textsf{B}})W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}\left(y_{ij}^{(dcr)}-X_{ij}^{(dcr)\top}\beta\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I_{C}^{2}J_{C}^{2}}\sum_{i,j}(1-W_{i}^{\textsf{B}})(1-W_{j}^{\textsf{S}})\left(y_{ij}^{(dcr)}-X_{ij}^{(dcr)\top}\beta\right)^{2},

where the double decentering is done within the respective groups
\gamma
. We can equivalently write the objective as

\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}^{2}J_{\gamma}^{2}}\sum_{(i,j)\in\gamma}\left(y_{ij}^{(dcr)}(\gamma)-X_{ij}^{(dcr)}(\gamma)^{\top}\beta\right)^{2},

(58)

from which it is apparent that the optimal
\beta
is given by
\tilde{Z}_{\dir}^{-1}\tilde{u}_{\dir}
. By the identifiability constraints, we have

\Delta_{\gamma}=\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}\sum_{(i,j)\in\gamma}(y_{ij}(\gamma)-X_{ij}^{\top}\beta).

Recalling that
\tau=\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}-\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}-\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}+\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}
we see that the fitted coefficient
\tau
is equal to
\hat{\tau}_{\dir}(\beta),
with
\beta
minimizing (
58
).
∎

Appendix C
Additional Details on Improved Theory for MRDs

In this section, we prove
Theorem

3.3
and present examples for which the result is stronger than the CLT of
[
23
]
. We can start by using a finite population central limit theorem in Wasserstein distance. The conclusion is slightly modified to suit our purposes.

Theorem C.1

(Adaptation of Theorem 5.1 in
[
11
]
)
.

Let
z_{1},\dots,z_{N}
be
N
real numbers, and
\mathcal{M}
be a uniformly random subset of
[N]
of size
n
. Let
S=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{M}}z_{k}.
Then

d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}\left(S-\mathbb{E}S\right),\textsf{N}(0,\Var(S))\right)\leq 9\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{N}|z_{k}-\overline{z}|^{3}}{\sum_{k=1}^{N}(z_{k}-\overline{z})^{2}}

Proof.

The result of Goldstein states that

d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}\left(\frac{S-\mathbb{E}S}{\sqrt{\Var(S)}}\right),\textsf{N}(0,1)\right)\leq 4\frac{n}{N}\frac{N-n}{N-1}\left(1+\frac{n}{N}\right)^{2}\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{N}|z_{k}-\overline{z}|^{3}}{\Var(S)^{3/2}}.

We can assume
n\leq N/2
without loss of generality since
-(S-\mathbb{E}S)=-\sum_{k\in\mathcal{M}}(z_{k}-\bar{z})=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{M}^{c}}(z_{k}-\bar{z})
. The variance formula for sampling without replacement gives
\Var(S)=\frac{n(N-n)}{N(N-1)}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(z_{i}-\bar{z})^{2}
. By scaling properties of Wasserstein distance,

\displaystyle d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}\left(S-\mathbb{E}S\right),\textsf{N}(0,\Var(S))\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\Var(S)}d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}\left(\frac{S-\mathbb{E}S}{\sqrt{\Var(S)}}\right),\textsf{N}(0,1)\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4\frac{n}{N}\frac{N-n}{N-1}\left(1+\frac{n}{N}\right)^{2}\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{N}|z_{k}-\overline{z}|^{3}}{\Var(S)}

\displaystyle=4\left(1+\frac{n}{N}\right)^{2}\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{N}|z_{k}-\overline{z}|^{3}}{\sum_{k=1}^{N}|z_{k}-\overline{z}|^{2}}.

The proof is completed by noting that
1+\frac{n}{N}\leq 3/2.

∎

With these preliminaries, we are able to now prove
Theorem

3.3
, which is restated here for convenience. The basic idea is to condition on
\mathscr{S}
, the collection of seller indicator variables
W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}
. Since the random variables in
\mathscr{S}
are discrete, conditioning amounts to considering all possible values of
(W_{j}^{\textsf{S}})_{j=1}^{J}\in\{0,1\}^{J},
so there are no measurability issues. Fixing
\mathscr{S}
, by independence of
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathscr{S}
,
Theorem

C.1
applies on the finite population
\frac{1}{I_{T}}\hat{\bar{y}}_{i,\bullet}
. The theorem shows that conditional on
\textsf{S},\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}
is approximately normal with mean
\mu_{\textsf{S}}:=\mathbb{E}\left[\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}\ |\ \mathscr{S}\right]
and conditional variance
\sigma^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}:=\Var\left(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}\ |\ \mathscr{S}\right)
. Under some conditions, one can show that
\sigma^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}
concentrates around its expectation
\mathbb{E}[\Var\left(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}\ |\ \mathscr{S}\right)].
Now, we can apply
Theorem

C.1
again to
\mu_{\textsf{S}}
, showing that
\mu_{\textsf{S}}
is approximately normal with mean
\mu
and variance
\Var(\mu_{\textsf{S}}).
Combining these facts roughly suggests that
\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}
is approximately normal with mean
\mu
and variance
\mathbb{E}\left[\Var\left(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}\ |\ \mathscr{S}\right)\right]+\Var(\mu_{\textsf{S}})=\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}).
The proof makes this precise.

See
3.3

Throughout this section, we will define the notation

d_{ij}:=y_{ij}-\bar{\bar{y}}

(59)

to denote the centered matrix
Y
.

Proof of
Theorem

3.3
.

Let
\mu:=\mathbb{E}\left[\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}\right],\mu_{\textsf{S}}:=\mathbb{E}\left[\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}\ |\ \mathscr{S}\right].
Similarly, let
\sigma^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}:=\Var\left(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}\ |\ \mathscr{S}\right)
and recall the notation
\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}}^{2}:=\Var\left(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}\right)
. Let
Z_{1}\sim\textsf{N}(0,1)
and independent of
\mathscr{S}
. By the law of total variance, we have
\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}[\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}^{2}]+\Var(\mu_{\textsf{S}}).
Then by triangle inequality,

\displaystyle d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}),\textsf{N}\left(\mu,\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}}^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}),\mathcal{L}\left(\mu_{\textsf{S}}+\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}Z_{1}\right)\right)

(60)

\displaystyle+d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}\left(\mu_{\textsf{S}}+\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}Z_{1}\right),\mathcal{L}\left(\mu_{\textsf{S}}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}\right]}Z_{1}\right)\right)

(61)

\displaystyle+d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}\left(\mu_{\textsf{S}}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}\right]}Z_{1}\right),\textsf{N}(\mu,\Var[\mu_{\textsf{S}}])*\textsf{N}\left(0,\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}^{2}\right]\right)\right)

(62)

Let us first bound (
60
). Conditioning on
(W_{j}^{\textsf{S}})_{j=1}^{J}=w
, we may write by
Lemma

C.2

d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}),\mathcal{L}\left(\mu_{\textsf{S}}+\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}Z_{1}\right)\right)\leq\mathbb{E}\left[d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}),\mathcal{L}\left(\mu_{\textsf{S}}+\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}Z_{1}\right)\ \bigg|\ (W_{j}^{\textsf{S}})_{j=1}^{J}=w\right)\right].

Apply
Theorem

C.1
, fixing
(W_{j}^{\textsf{S}})_{j=1}^{J}
with
z_{i}=\frac{1}{I_{T}}\hat{\bar{y}}_{i,\bullet}
, to the sum
\frac{1}{I_{T}}\sum_{i}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}\hat{\bar{y}}_{i,\bullet}
. The conditional average and variance we can calculate explicitly as

\displaystyle\mu_{\textsf{S}}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}[S|\mathscr{S}]=\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\hat{\bar{y}}_{i,\bullet}

\displaystyle\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}^{2}

\displaystyle:=\Var[S|\mathscr{S}]=\left(\frac{1}{I_{T}}-\frac{1}{I}\right)\frac{1}{I-1}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\left(\hat{\bar{y}}_{i,\bullet}-\mu_{\textsf{S}}\right)^{2}.

Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mu_{\textsf{S}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[S]=\bar{\bar{y}}.

\displaystyle\Var[\mu_{\textsf{S}}]

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{J_{T}}-\frac{1}{J}\right)\frac{1}{J-1}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left(\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right)^{2}=\left(\frac{1}{J_{T}}-\frac{1}{J}\right)\omega^{\textsf{S}}(y)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\Var[S|\mathscr{S}]]

\displaystyle=\Var[S]-\Var[\mu_{\textsf{S}}]=\left(\frac{1}{I_{T}}-\frac{1}{I}\right)\omega^{\textsf{B}}(y)+\left(\frac{1}{I_{T}}-\frac{1}{I}\right)\left(\frac{1}{J_{T}}-\frac{1}{J}\right)\omega^{\textsf{BS}}(y).

Theorem

C.1
gives

d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}),\mathcal{L}\left(\mu_{\textsf{S}}+\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}Z_{1}\right)\ \bigg|\ (W_{j}^{\textsf{S}})_{j=1}^{J}=w\right)\leq\frac{9}{I_{T}}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{I}\left|\hat{\bar{y}}_{i,\bullet}-\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{I}\hat{\bar{y}}_{i^{\prime},\bullet}\right|^{3}}{\sum_{i=1}^{I}\left(\hat{\bar{y}}_{i,\bullet}-\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{I}\hat{\bar{y}}_{i^{\prime},\bullet}\right)^{2}},

so

d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}),\mathcal{L}\left(\mu_{\textsf{S}}+\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}Z_{1}\right)\right)\leq\frac{9}{I_{T}}\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{i=1}^{I}\left|\hat{\bar{y}}_{i,\bullet}-\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{I}\hat{\bar{y}}_{i^{\prime},\bullet}\right|\right].

We can bound the right hand side by the expected suprema of sub-Gaussian random variables via
Lemma

D.1
, after removing the expected value:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{i}\left|\hat{\bar{y}}_{i,\bullet}-\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{I}\hat{\bar{y}}_{i^{\prime},\bullet}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\max_{i}\left|\hat{\bar{y}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{i=1}^{I}\left|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right|+\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{i}\left|\hat{\bar{y}}_{i,\bullet}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{i=1}^{I}\left|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right|+\sqrt{\log I}\max_{i}\sqrt{\Var\hat{\bar{y}}_{i,\bullet}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{i=1}^{I}\left|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right|+\sqrt{\frac{\log I}{J}}\max_{i,j}|y_{ij}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}|.

Next, consider the second term (
61
). First, if we condition on a particular realization
(W_{j}^{\textsf{S}})_{j=1}^{J}=w
, then the obvious coupling gives

d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}\left(\mu_{\textsf{S}}+\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}Z_{1}\right),\mathcal{L}\left(\mu_{\textsf{S}}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}\right]}Z_{1}\right)\ \bigg|\ (W_{j}^{\textsf{S}})_{j=1}^{J}=w\right)\leq\left|\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}-\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}\right]}\right|\mathbb{E}[|Z_{1}|].

Thus, by
Lemma

C.2
, we have

d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}\left(\mu_{\textsf{S}}+\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}Z_{1}\right),\mathcal{L}\left(\mu_{\textsf{S}}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}\right]}Z_{1}\right)\right)\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left|\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}-\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}\right]}\right|.

We upper bound this using vector concentration for sampling without replacement, using
Lemma

C.1
to obtain

\mathbb{E}\left|\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}-\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}\right]}\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{I}\sqrt{\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}}\norm{d_{ij}}_{\text{op}}}+\frac{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{1/2}}{\sqrt{I}}.

Finally, for the third term (
62
), rewrite
\mu_{\textsf{S}}=\frac{1}{J_{T}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}
. We apply
Lemma

C.4
and
Theorem

C.1
again to give the upper bound

\displaystyle d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}\left(\mu_{\textsf{S}}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}\right]}Z_{1}\right),\textsf{N}(\mu,\Var[\mu_{\textsf{S}}])*\textsf{N}\left(0,\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}^{2}\right]\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}(\mu_{\textsf{S}}),\textsf{N}(\mu,\Var[\mu_{\textsf{S}}])\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{9}{J_{T}}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left|\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right|^{3}}{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left(\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right)^{2}}.

Putting the bounds together and simplifying using e.g.
I_{T}\gtrsim I
, we get

\displaystyle d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}},\textsf{N}\left(\mu,\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}}^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{I}\max_{i=1}^{I}\left|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right|+\frac{\sqrt{\log I}}{I^{3/2}}\max_{i,j}|y_{ij}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}|

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{I}\sqrt{\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}}\norm{d_{ij}}_{\text{op}}}+\frac{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{1/2}}{\sqrt{I}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{J}\max_{j=1}^{J}\left|\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right|.

Dividing through by the square root of the variance, we obtain

\displaystyle d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}\left(\frac{\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}-\mu}{\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}}}\right),\textsf{N}\left(0,1\right)\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{I}}+\frac{1}{I}\frac{\max_{i=1}^{I}\left|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right|}{\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}}}+\frac{1}{J}\frac{\max_{j=1}^{J}\left|\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right|}{\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\sqrt{\log I}}{I^{3/2}}\frac{\max_{i,j}|y_{ij}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}|}{\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}}}+\left(\frac{1}{I^{2}}\frac{\norm{y_{ij} - \dbar{y}}_{\text{op}}}{\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}}}\right)^{1/2}.

∎

Lemma C.1
.

We have the bound

\mathbb{E}\left|\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}-\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}\right]}\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{I}\sqrt{\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}}\norm{d_{ij}}_{\operatorname{op}}}+\frac{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{1/2}}{\sqrt{I}}.

Proof.

Recall that

\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}^{2}\propto\frac{1}{I}\left(\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\hat{\bar{y}}_{i,\bullet}^{2}-\mu_{\textsf{S}}^{2}\right).

Since
\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}
remains unchanged when shifting
y_{ij}
by a constant, we can replace
y_{ij}
by
d_{ij}=y_{ij}-\bar{\bar{y}}.
Let
m_{2,\textsf{S}}:=\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\hat{\bar{d}}_{i,\bullet}^{2}
, so
I\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}^{2}\propto m_{2,\textsf{S}}-\mu_{\textsf{S}}^{2}
. Then by the simple inequality
\left|\sqrt{x}-\sqrt{y}\right|\leq\sqrt{|x-y|}
,

\mathbb{E}\left|\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}-\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}\right]}\right|\leq\mathbb{E}\sqrt{\left|\sigma^{2}_{\textsf{S}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}\right]\right|}.

Applying Jensen’s inequality to the right-hand side above, then using the triangle inequality and
the elementary bound
\sqrt{x+y}\lesssim\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y}
, we have

\sqrt{I}\mathbb{E}\left|\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}-\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}\right]}\right|\leq\sqrt{I\mathbb{E}|\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}-\mathbb{E}[\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}]|}\lesssim\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left|m_{2,\textsf{S}}-\mathbb{E}[m_{2,\textsf{S}}]\right|}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left|\mu_{\textsf{S}}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[\mu_{\textsf{S}}^{2}]\right|}.

(63)

We will upper bound both these quantities using subgaussianity and
Lemma

C.3
. Firstly,
\sqrt{Im_{2,\textsf{S}}}=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{I}\hat{\bar{d}}_{i,\bullet}^{2}}
is subgaussian after centering using
Lemma

D.2
. Apply the lemma taking
u_{j}
to be the
J
vectors
\frac{1}{J_{T}}(d_{1,j},\dots,d_{I,j})
, so
\sum_{j}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}u_{j}=(\hat{\bar{d}}_{1,\bullet}^{2},\ldots,\hat{\bar{d}}_{I,\bullet}^{2})
and
\|\sum_{j}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}u_{j}\|_{2}^{2}=Im_{2,\textsf{S}}
. Thus,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sqrt{Im_{2,\textsf{S}}}-\mathbb{E}\sqrt{Im_{2,\textsf{S}}}\right|\geq t\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{J_{T}^{2}t^{2}}{8\norm{(d_{ij})}_{\text{op}}^{2}}\right):

\sqrt{m_{2,\textsf{S}}}-\mathbb{E}\sqrt{m_{2,\textsf{S}}}
is subgaussian with parameter
\norm{(d_{ij})}_{\text{op}}/I^{3/2}
, up to absolute constants. Applying
Lemma

C.3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|m_{2,\textsf{S}}-\mathbb{E}[m_{2,\textsf{S}}]\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\norm{(d_{ij})}_{\text{op}}^{2}}{I^{3}}+\frac{\norm{(d_{ij})}_{\text{op}}}{I^{3/2}}\left|\mathbb{E}[\sqrt{m_{2,\textsf{S}}}]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\norm{(d_{ij})}_{\text{op}}^{2}}{I^{3}}+\frac{\norm{(d_{ij})}_{\text{op}}}{I^{3/2}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[m_{2,\textsf{S}}]}.

Note the bounds
\norm{(d_{ij})}_{\text{op}}\leq\norm{(d_{ij})}_{\text{F}}\leq I^{2}\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}}
by
Lemma

C.10
along with

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[m_{2,\textsf{S}}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\bar{d}}_{i,\bullet}^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i}\left(\frac{1}{I^{2}}\sum_{j}(d_{ij}-\overline{d}_{i,\bullet})^{2}+\overline{d}_{i,\bullet}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{I^{3}}\sum_{i,j}(y_{ij}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet})^{2}+\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i}\overline{d}_{i,\bullet}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{I^{3}}\sum_{i,j}(\delta_{ij}^{\textsf{B}\textsf{S}}+\delta_{j}^{\textsf{S}})^{2}+\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i}\overline{d}_{i,\bullet}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{I}(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}+\omega^{\textsf{S}})+\omega^{\textsf{B}}.

Thus
\mathbb{E}[m_{2,\textsf{S}}]\lesssim I\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}}
. Putting these bounds together, we arrive at

\mathbb{E}\left|m_{2,\textsf{S}}-\mathbb{E}[m_{2,\textsf{S}}]\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{I}\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}}\norm{d_{ij}}_{\text{op}}.

Next, a direct application of
Lemma

D.1
shows that
\mu_{\textsf{S}}-\mathbb{E}[\mu_{\textsf{S}}]
is subgaussian with parameter
\sqrt{\frac{1}{J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}}
. Applying
Lemma

C.3
, we find

\mathbb{E}\left|\mu_{\textsf{S}}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[\mu_{\textsf{S}}^{2}]\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}}\left|\mathbb{E}[\mu_{\textsf{S}}]\right|\lesssim\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}}^{2},

which follows since
\left|\mathbb{E}[\mu_{\textsf{S}}]\right|=0
by the assumption that
y_{ij}
can be replaced by
d_{ij}.
Putting these bounds together with (
63
), we obtain

\mathbb{E}\left|\sigma_{\mathscr{S}}-\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}\right]}\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{I}\sqrt{\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}}\norm{d_{ij}}_{\text{op}}}+\frac{\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}}}{\sqrt{I}}.

∎

C.1
Examples

We explore simple conditions and data generating processes under which we can expect asymptotic normality.

Corollary C.1
.

Suppose
I,J\rightarrow\infty,I_{T}/I,J_{T}/J=O(1)
and

\max\left(\frac{1}{I}\max_{i=1}^{I}|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}|,\frac{1}{J}\max_{j=1}^{J}|\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}-\bar{\bar{y}}|,\frac{\sqrt{\log I}}{I^{3/2}}\max_{ij}|y_{ij}-\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}|,\frac{1}{I^{2}}\norm{y_{ij} - \dbar{y}}_{\text{op}}\right)=o(\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}})

(64)

Then

\frac{\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}-\bar{\bar{y}}}{\sigma_{\textsf{Tot}}}\Rightarrow\textsf{N}(0,1).

These are the primitive conditions for the right hand side of
Theorem

3.3
to go to zero. Next, we may apply the corollary above to obtain CLTs for MRD estimators.

See
3.1

Proof.

First, notice that for each empirical average
\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}_{\gamma}
, we may write

\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}_{\gamma}=\frac{1}{I_{T}J_{T}}\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}z_{ij}(\gamma)

for some “pseudo”-potential outcomes
z_{ij}.
Thus,

\widehat{\tau}_{\mathbf{c}}=\frac{1}{I_{T}J_{T}}\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}(\sum_{\gamma}c_{\gamma}z_{ij}(\gamma))

(65)

Explicitly,

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}(1-W_{i}^{\textsf{B}})(1-W_{j}^{\textsf{S}})y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})=\sum_{i,j}y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})-\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})-\sum_{i,j}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})+\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})

\displaystyle=\frac{IJ}{I_{C}J_{C}}\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}\bar{\bar{y}}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})-\frac{J}{J_{T}}\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})-\frac{I}{I_{T}}\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})+\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}\left(\frac{IJ}{I_{C}J_{C}}\bar{\bar{y}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}-\frac{J}{J_{T}}\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})-\frac{I}{I_{T}}\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})+y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})\right).

Similar computations can be carried out for the other groups.

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}(1-W_{j}^{\textsf{S}})y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}})=\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}})-\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}})

\displaystyle=\frac{J}{J_{T}}\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}({\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}})-\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}\left(\frac{J}{J_{T}}\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}({\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}})-y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}})\right).

Finally,

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}(1-W_{i}^{\textsf{B}})W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}})=\sum_{i,j}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}})-\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}})

\displaystyle=\frac{I}{I_{T}}\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}})-\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}\left(\frac{I}{I_{T}}\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}})-y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}})\right).

Let
\gamma_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}:=I_{T}J_{T}/I_{C}I_{C},\gamma_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}:=J_{T}/J_{C},\gamma_{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}:=I_{T}/I_{C}
. Thus,

\displaystyle z_{ij}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}})

\displaystyle:=y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}})

(66)

\displaystyle z_{ij}({\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}})

\displaystyle:=\gamma_{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}\frac{I}{I_{T}}\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}({\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}})-\gamma_{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}})

(67)

\displaystyle z_{ij}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}})

\displaystyle:=\gamma_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}\frac{J}{J_{T}}\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}})-\gamma_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}})

(68)

\displaystyle z_{ij}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})

\displaystyle:=\gamma_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}\frac{IJ}{I_{C}J_{C}}\bar{\bar{y}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}-\gamma_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}\frac{J}{J_{T}}\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})-\gamma_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}\frac{I}{I_{T}}\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})+\gamma_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}).

(69)

Inspecting these formulas, it is evident that for each
\gamma
,

\displaystyle\left|\overline{z}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{z}}_{\gamma}\right|\lesssim\left|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right|

\displaystyle\left|\overline{z}_{\bullet,j}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{z}}_{\gamma}\right|\lesssim\left|\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right|

\displaystyle\left|z_{ij}(\gamma)-\overline{z}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma)\right|\lesssim\left|y_{ij}(\gamma)-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma)\right|+\left|\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right|

\displaystyle\left|z_{ij}(\gamma)-\overline{z}_{\bullet,j}(\gamma)\right|\lesssim\left|y_{ij}(\gamma)-\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}(\gamma)\right|+\left|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right|.

Also, note that by the triangle inequality for the operator norm,

\norm{z_{ij}(\gamma) - \dbar{z}_\gamma}_{\text{op}}\lesssim\norm{y_{ij}(\gamma) - \dbar{y}_\gamma}_{\text{op}}+\norm{\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma) - \dbar{y}_\gamma}_{\text{op}}+\norm{\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}(\gamma) - \dbar{y}_\gamma}_{\text{op}}.

Further,
\norm{\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma) - \dbar{y}_\gamma}_{\text{op}}\lesssim I\max_{i}\left|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right|
and
\norm{\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}(\gamma) - \dbar{y}_\gamma}_{\text{op}}\lesssim J\max_{j}\left|\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right|
.
Using these relations and the given assumption, it is not difficult to check (
64
) for the pseudo-outcomes
\sum_{\gamma}c_{\gamma}z_{ij}(\gamma)
. Thus
Corollary

C.1
applies.
∎

Another condition could just bound the residuals of the pseudo-outcomes directly in terms of the variance. Our theorem generalizes the MRD of
[
23
]
in some ways.

Corollary C.2
.

Suppose that
y_{ij}(\gamma)
are all bounded by
C
, for
\gamma\in\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}\}
and suppose
I^{2}\Var(\widehat{\tau}_{\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow\infty.
Then

\frac{\widehat{\tau}_{\mathbf{c}}-\tau_{\mathbf{c}}}{\Var(\widehat{\tau}_{\mathbf{c}})^{1/2}}\Rightarrow\textsf{N}(0,1).

Proof.

By the argument of Corollary
3.1
, we may write

\widehat{\tau}_{\mathbf{c}}=\frac{1}{I_{T}J_{T}}\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}z^{\prime}_{ij}

for some quantities
z^{\prime}_{ij}
stated explicitly in (
66
)-(
69
). By the boundedness assumption,
|z^{\prime}_{ij}|\leq C^{\prime}
. Further, by the inequality
\norm{M}_{\operatorname{op}}\lesssim\sqrt{IJ}\max_{ij}|M_{ij}|
for any
I\times J
matrix
M
, we see that
\norm{z_{ij}' - \dbar{z}'}_{\operatorname{op}}\leq C^{\prime}I
. Then by assumption
I^{-2}\norm{z_{ij}' - \dbar{z}'}_{\operatorname{op}}=o_{p}(\Var(\widehat{\tau}_{\mathbf{c}})^{1/2})
. Thus,
Corollary

C.1
implies the desired weak convergence.
∎

Example C.1

(IID normal superpopulation)
.

Consider potential outcome matrices which are a single realization from the data generating process

y_{ij}(\gamma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.i.d.}}{{\sim}}\sf{N}(\mu_{\gamma},1)

(70)

for some constants
\mu_{\gamma}
. Then
\omega^{\textsf{B}}(y_{ij}(\gamma))=\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}(\bar{y}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma})^{2}=\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}O(1/\sqrt{J})^{2}=O(1/J)
. On the other hand,
\omega^{\textsf{BS}}(y_{ij}(\gamma))=O(1).
Therefore,
\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}_{\gamma})=\Theta(1/IJ).
Similarly, one can also show that
\Var(\widehat{\tau}_{c})=\Theta(1/IJ)
. This does not fit into the framework of the CLT of
[
23
]
, since the potential outcomes are unbounded and the variance decays too quickly. In contrast, our results can establish asymptotic normality in this case.

Using
Corollary

3.1
it suffices to check (
47
). In particular, for the data generating process in (
70
), the following estimates hold with high probability:

\displaystyle\max_{i=1}^{I}|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}|=O(\sqrt{J^{-1}\log I})

\displaystyle\max_{j=1}^{J}|\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}|=O(\sqrt{I^{-1}\log J})

\displaystyle\max_{i,j}|y_{ij}(\gamma)-\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}(\gamma)|=O(\sqrt{\log IJ})

\displaystyle\norm{y_{ij}(\gamma) - \dbar{y}_\gamma}_{\operatorname{op}}=O(\sqrt{I}).

The last claim is a standard fact; see e.g.
[
28
]
. Thus (
47
) is satisfied and the CLT holds.

Example C.2

(Sparse Uniform Subgraph)
.

Suppose that
y_{ij}(\gamma)\sim X_{i}Y_{j}Z_{ij}
where
X_{i},Y_{j}\overset{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}\mathrm{Ber}(1/2)
and
Z_{ij}\overset{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}\mathrm{Ber}(4\mu)
for
\frac{1}{2}\gg\mu\gg(I\wedge J)^{-1}
. In the limit as
\mu\downarrow 0
, we can show that up to logarithmic factors,

\Var\{\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}_{\gamma}|X,Y,Z\}\asymp\frac{\mu^{2}}{I}+\frac{\mu^{2}}{J}+\frac{\mu}{IJ}.

Meanwhile, with high probability,

\displaystyle\max_{i=1}^{I}|\bar{y}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}|=O(\sqrt{\mu J^{-1}}\log I)

\displaystyle\max_{j=1}^{J}|\bar{y}_{\bullet,j}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}|=O(\sqrt{\mu I^{-1}}\log J)

\displaystyle\max_{i,j}|y_{ij}(\gamma)-\bar{y}_{\bullet,j}(\gamma)|=O(1)

\displaystyle\norm{y_{ij}(\gamma) - \dbar{y}_\gamma}_{\operatorname{op}}=O\left([(I+J)\mu]^{\frac{1}{2}}+(IJ\mu)^{\frac{1}{4}}\right).

The first two estimates follow from Bernstein’s inequality for bounded random variables, while the last follows from applying Latala’s theorem conditional upon
(X,Y)
. Thus Eq. (
47
) is satisfied and the CLT holds whenever
\mu\gg(I\wedge J)^{-1}
. This is a major improvement over
[
23
]
, which requires
\mu\gg(I\wedge J)^{-1/2}
.

Example C.3

(Sparse Heavy Tailed)
.

We consider another example different from those presented in the main text. Suppose that
y_{ij}(\gamma)\sim X_{i}Y_{j}Z_{ij}
where
X_{i},Y_{j}\overset{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}\mathrm{Ber}(\sqrt{2\mu})
and
Z_{ij}\overset{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}\mathrm{Ber}(1/2)
for
\frac{1}{2}\gg\mu\gg(I\wedge J)^{-1}
.
In the limit as
\mu\downarrow 0
, we can show that up to logarithmic factors,

\Var\{\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}_{\gamma}|X,Y,Z\}\asymp\frac{\mu}{I}+\frac{\mu}{J}+\frac{\mu}{IJ}.

To see this, note that with very high probability
I^{-1}\sum_{i}X_{i},J^{-1}\sum_{j}Y_{j}
are of order
\sqrt{\mu}
and
\bar{\bar{y}}
is of order
\mu
.
Meanwhile, with high probability,

\displaystyle\max_{i=1}^{I}|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}|=O(1)

\displaystyle\max_{j=1}^{J}|\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}|=O(1)

\displaystyle\max_{i,j}|y_{ij}(\gamma)-\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}(\gamma)|=O(1)

\displaystyle\norm{y_{ij}(\gamma) - \dbar{y}_\gamma}_{\operatorname{op}}=O\left([\sqrt{I}+\sqrt{J}]\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}\right).

Thus, once again, convergence holds when
\mu\gg(I\wedge J)^{-1}
.

C.2
Auxiliary Results for CLT

Lemma C.2

(Tensorization of Wasserstein Distance)
.

Consider the mixture distributions
\sum_{i}\pi_{i}F_{i},\sum_{i}\pi_{i}G_{i}
for
\sum_{i}\pi_{i}=1
and
\pi_{i}\geq 0.

(F_{i})_{i},(G_{i})_{i}
are distributions. Then

d_{W}\left(\sum_{i}\pi_{i}F_{i},\sum_{i}\pi_{i}G_{i}\right)\leq\sum_{i}\pi_{i}d_{W}\left(F_{i},G_{i}\right).

Proof.

Let
(X_{i},Y_{i})
be couplings between
F_{i},G_{i}
for all
i
. Let
P
be a random variable that equals
i
, for
i=1,\dots,n
, with probability
\pi_{i}
, independent of
(X_{i},Y_{i})
. Then
(X_{P},Y_{P})
is a coupling between
\sum_{i}\pi_{i}F_{i},\sum_{i}\pi_{i}G_{i}
. Thus,
\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{P}-Y_{P}|\right]\leq\sum_{i}\pi_{i}\mathbb{E}[|X_{i}-Y_{i}|]
, which implies that

d_{W}\left(\sum_{i}\pi_{i}F_{i},\sum_{i}\pi_{i}G_{i}\right)\leq\sum_{i}\pi_{i}\mathbb{E}[|X_{i}-Y_{i}|].

Take the infimum over the right hand side to conclude the desired property.
∎

Lemma C.3
.

Consider a random variable
X
and set
\mu:=\mathbb{E}X
. Suppose that
X-\mu
is subgaussian with parameter
\sigma
. Then

\mathbb{E}\left|X^{2}-\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]\right|\lesssim\sigma^{2}+\sigma\left|\mu\right|.

Proof.

By properties of sub-Gaussian random variables,
\mathbb{E}[(X-\mu)^{2}]\lesssim\sigma^{2}
(see e.g.
[
28
]
Proposition 2.5.2). Note the expansion
X^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left[X^{2}\right]=(X-\mu)^{2}-\Var(X)+2\mu(X-\mu)
. Taking absolute values and expectations, we obtain
\mathbb{E}\left|X^{2}-\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]\right|\leq 2\Var(X)+2|\mu|\mathbb{E}\left|X-\mu\right|
. Using subgaussianity again, we arrive at
\mathbb{E}\left|X^{2}-\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]\right|\lesssim\sigma^{2}+\sigma\left|\mu\right|
as desired.
∎

Lemma C.4
.

Consider independent random variables
X,Y
with laws
\mathcal{L}_{X},\mathcal{L}_{Y}
. Let
\mathcal{G}
be another distribution. Then

d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}_{X}*\mathcal{L}_{Y},\mathcal{G}*\mathcal{L}_{Y}\right)\leq d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}_{X},\mathcal{G}\right).

Proof.

Let
(X,X^{\prime})
be a coupling of
\mathcal{L}_{X}
and
\mathcal{G}.
Draw
Y\sim\mathcal{L}_{Y}
independent of
X,X^{\prime}
. Then
\left(X+Y,X^{\prime}+Y\right)
is a coupling of
\mathcal{L}_{X}*\mathcal{L}_{y},\mathcal{G}*\mathcal{L}_{Y}
. By the coupling definition of Wasserstein distance,
d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}_{X}*\mathcal{L}_{Y},\mathcal{G}*\mathcal{L}_{Y}\right)\leq\mathbb{E}|X-X^{\prime}|
. Taking the infimum of all couplings between
\mathcal{L}_{X},G
, yields
d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}_{X}*\mathcal{L}_{Y},\mathcal{G}*\mathcal{L}_{Y}\right)\leq d_{W}\left(\mathcal{L}_{X},\mathcal{G}\right)
.
∎

C.3
Results for Conservative Variance Estimation

In this section, we will prove consistency of the conservative variance estimator.
See
3.4

Proof.

We claim that it suffices to show that
\widehat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}=(1+o_{p}(1))\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}_{\gamma})
. Recall the elementary facts that (i)
\sqrt{1+o_{p}(1)}=1+o_{p}(1)
and (ii) for any nonnegative random variables
X,Y,

X(1+o_{p}(1))+Y(1+o_{p}(1))=(X+Y)(1+o_{p}(1)).
Furthermore, if
X=(1+o_{p}(1))Y
for
Y
nonnegative, then the continuous mapping theorem implies that
X^{+}=(1+o_{p}(1))Y.
Assembling the pieces,

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}|c_{\gamma}|\sqrt{\widehat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}^{+}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}|c_{\gamma}|\sqrt{(1+o_{p}(1))\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}_{\gamma})}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}(1+o_{p}(1))|c_{\gamma}|\sqrt{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}_{\gamma})}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}|c_{\gamma}|\sqrt{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}_{\gamma})}\right)^{2}(1+o_{p}(1)),

which would imply
\frac{\widehat{V}_{\mathbf{c}}}{V_{\mathbf{c}}}\xrightarrow{p}1.
Therefore in the rest of this proof, we focus on showing that
\widehat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}=(1+o_{p}(1))\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}_{\gamma})
. Recalling that
\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}]=\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}_{\gamma})
we need only show that

\frac{\Var(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\gamma})}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}_{\gamma})^{2}}\rightarrow 0.

We will focus on each term in the expression
\widehat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}
. Recalling the definition of
\alpha_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}}
and
\alpha_{\gamma}^{\textsf{S}}
defined in (
39
). Clearly,
1-\alpha^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}-\alpha^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma}+\alpha^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}\alpha^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma},1-\alpha^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma},1-\alpha^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma}
are all bounded away from zero, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\frac{\Var(\alpha^{\textsf{B}}\widehat{\Sigma}^{\textsf{B}})}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{2}}\rightarrow 0

(71)

\displaystyle\frac{\Var(\alpha^{\textsf{S}}\widehat{\Sigma}^{\textsf{S}})}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{2}}\rightarrow 0

(72)

\displaystyle\frac{\Var(\alpha^{\textsf{B}}\alpha^{\textsf{S}}\widehat{\Sigma}^{\textsf{BS}})}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{2}}\rightarrow 0

(73)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I^{4}}\frac{\Var\left(\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}\sum_{(i,j)\in\gamma}\left(y_{ij}(\gamma)-\widehat{\overline{y}}_{\bullet,j}(\gamma)\right)^{2}\right)}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{2}}\rightarrow 0

(74)

Showing the first three asymptotic statements is done in
Proposition

C.1
and
Proposition

C.2
. For (
74
), elementary algebra shows that

\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}\sum_{i,j\in\gamma}\left(y_{ij}(\gamma)-\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma)\right)^{2}=\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}\sum_{i,j\in\gamma}\left(y_{ij}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\gamma}\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right)^{2}.

Lemma

C.6
and
Lemma

C.7
show that the variance of both of these terms, divided by
I^{4}
, is
o(\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{2})
. The last term in (
40
) is symmetric with the fourth term, which is handled by (
74
).

∎

As the corresponding proofs are similar, throughout the section we will assume
\gamma={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}
and also omit the dependence, where clear.

Proposition C.1
.

Under the assumptions of
Theorem

3.4
,

\frac{\Var(\alpha^{\textsf{B}}\widehat{\Sigma}^{\textsf{B}})}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{2}}\rightarrow 0,\quad\frac{\Var(\alpha^{\textsf{S}}\widehat{\Sigma}^{\textsf{S}})}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{2}}\rightarrow 0.

Proof.

By symmetry we will focus on the buyer term. Note first that Lemma A.12 of
[
23
]
, or alternatively simple algebra, shows that

\widehat{\Sigma}^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}=\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right)^{2}-\left(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}_{\gamma}-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right)^{2}.

Using
Lemma

C.11
, it suffices to bound the variance of the individual terms. This is accomplished in
Lemmas

C.5
and
C.6
.

∎

Proposition C.2
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem 6.2, we have

\frac{\Var(\alpha^{\textsf{B}}\alpha^{\textsf{S}}\widehat{\Sigma}^{\textsf{BS}})}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{2}}\rightarrow 0

Proof.

Lemma A.15 of
[
23
]
shows

\widehat{\Sigma}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}=\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma},j\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}}\left(y_{ij}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}}\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{\bullet,j}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right)^{2}+\left(\widehat{\bar{\bar{y}}}_{\gamma}-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right)^{2}.

Using
Lemma

C.11
, it suffices to bound the variance of the individual terms.
Lemmas

C.5
,
C.6
and
C.7
in tandem show that each of the terms are
o(I^{4}\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{2})
as desired.
∎

C.4
Lemmas for Proofs

Lemma C.5
.

\frac{1}{I^{2}}\frac{\Var\left[\left(\widehat{\bar{\bar{y}}}_{\gamma}-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right)^{2}\right]}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{2}}\rightarrow 0

Proof.

Bounding the variance by the second moment and using
Lemma

D.5
, we conclude that

\Var\left[\left(\widehat{\bar{\bar{y}}}_{\gamma}-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right)^{2}\right]\leq C\Var\left(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}\right)^{2}.

Thus

\frac{\Var\left[\alpha^{\textsf{B}}\left(\widehat{\bar{\bar{y}}}_{\gamma}-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right)^{2}\right]}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{2}}\leq\frac{C}{I^{2}}.

∎

Lemma C.6
.

\frac{1}{I^{2}}\frac{\Var\left[\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right)^{2}\right]}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{2}}\rightarrow 0.

Proof.

The ANOVA decomposition lets us compute this variance as

\begin{split}&\Var\left[\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right)^{2}\right].\\
+&\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{C}{I(I-1)}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\left[\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i^{\prime}}\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i^{\prime},\bullet}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right)^{2}\right]^{2}\right].\end{split}

(75)

For the first term, apply
Lemma

D.2
taking the vectors
u_{j}:=\frac{1}{J_{T}}\left(d_{1j},\dots,d_{Ij}\right)^{\top}
. Then
\sum_{j}^{J}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}u_{j}=\left(\hat{\bar{d}}_{i,\bullet}\right)_{i}^{\top}
. Then let

D:=\norm{\left( \eavg{d}_{i,\bullet}\right)_i}=\left(\sum_{i}\hat{\bar{d}}_{i,\bullet}^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

Lemma

D.2
gives that
D-\mathbb{E}D
is a sub-Gaussian random variable with sub-Gaussian norm
\sigma_{D}:=\frac{1}{J_{T}}\norm{(d_{ij})_{ij}}_{\text{op}}.
Applying
Lemma

C.8
we find that

\displaystyle\Var\left[\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right)^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{I^{2}}\Var(D^{2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{I^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[(D-\mathbb{E}D)^{4}\right]+4\frac{\mathbb{E}[D]}{I^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[|D-\mathbb{E}D|^{3}\right]+4\frac{\mathbb{E}[D]^{2}}{I^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[(D-\mathbb{E}D)^{2}\right]

Subgaussian bounds on the moments give us the upper bound, up to an absolute constant scaling factor,

\frac{1}{I^{6}}\norm{(d_{ij})}^{4}_{\text{op}}+\frac{1}{I^{5}}\mathbb{E}[D]\norm{(d_{ij})}^{3}_{\text{op}}+\frac{1}{I^{4}}\mathbb{E}[D]^{2}\norm{(d_{ij})}^{2}_{\text{op}}

(76)

We have
\frac{1}{I^{4}}\norm{(d_{ij})}^{2}_{F}\lesssim\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})
and
\frac{1}{I^{2}}\mathbb{E}[D^{2}]\lesssim\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})
by
Lemma

C.10
. Using
\mathbb{E}[D]\leq\mathbb{E}[D^{2}]^{1/2}
and
\norm{(d_{ij})}_{\text{op}}\leq\norm{(d_{ij})}_{F}
then yields for the first term in (
76
)

\frac{1}{I^{6}}\norm{(d_{ij})}^{4}_{\text{op}}\leq\frac{1}{I^{6}}\norm{(d_{ij})}^{2}_{F}\norm{(d_{ij})}^{2}_{\text{op}}\lesssim\frac{1}{I^{2}}\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})\norm{(d_{ij})}_{\text{op}}^{2}

For the second term in (
76
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I^{5}}\mathbb{E}[D]\norm{(d_{ij})}^{3}_{\text{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{I^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{I^{2}}\mathbb{E}[D^{2}]}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{I^{2}}\norm{(d_{ij})}_{F}\right)\norm{(d_{ij})}_{\text{op}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{I^{2}}\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})\norm{(d_{ij})}_{\text{op}}^{2}

For the third term in (
76
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I^{4}}\mathbb{E}[D]^{2}\norm{(d_{ij})}^{2}_{\text{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{I^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{I^{2}}\mathbb{E}[D^{2}]\right)\norm{(d_{ij})}_{\text{op}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{I^{2}}\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})\norm{(d_{ij})}_{\text{op}}^{2}.

Summarizing the established bounds, we see that

\frac{1}{I^{2}}\frac{\Var\left[\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right)^{2}\right]}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{2}}\leq\frac{C}{I^{4}}\frac{\norm{(d_{ij})}_{\text{op}}^{2}}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})},

which goes to zero by assumption.

For the second term of (
75
), upper bound it by

\frac{C}{I^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right)^{4}\right]

Rewrite
\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right)=\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i,\bullet}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}\right)+\left(\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right)
and expand, yielding

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i,\bullet}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}\right)^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{I^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right)^{4}\right]

(77)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i,\bullet}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}\right)^{3}\right]\left(\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right)

(78)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i,\bullet}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}\right)^{2}\right]\left(\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right)^{2}

(79)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\left(\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right)^{4}

(80)

We bound each of these terms separately.

1.

For
(
77
)
,
Lemma

D.1
gives us the upper bound

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\Var(\hat{\bar{y}}_{i,\bullet})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{I^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\left[\frac{1}{J^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}(y_{ij}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet})^{2}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{I^{5}}\sum_{i,j}(y_{ij}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet})^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{I^{5}}\sum_{i,j}(y_{ij}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet})^{2}\cdot\max|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-y_{ij}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{I}\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})\cdot\max|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-y_{ij}|^{2}

2.

For
(
78
)
, applying the same subgaussian moment bound and Jensen’s inequality yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i,\bullet}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}\right)^{3}\right]\left(\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max_{i}|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}|\frac{1}{I^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\left[\frac{1}{J^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}(y_{ij}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet})^{2}\right]^{3/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max_{i}|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}|\frac{1}{I^{2}J^{3/2}J}\sum_{i,j}|y_{ij}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}|^{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I^{1/2}}\max_{i}|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}|\max_{ij}|y_{ij}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}|\Var[\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}].

3.
