For
(
79
)
apply the same logic:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i,\bullet}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}\right)^{2}\right]\left(\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max_{i}|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}|^{2}\frac{1}{I^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\left[\frac{1}{J^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}(y_{ij}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max_{i}|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}|^{2}\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})

4.

For
(
80
)
, we can easily upper bound the term by
\frac{1}{I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}(y_{ij})\max_{i}|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}|^{2}\leq\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})\max_{i}|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}|^{2}
.

Combining the individual bounds gives the upper bound

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I^{2}}\frac{I^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widehat{\overline{y}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right)^{4}\right]}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{I^{3}}\frac{\max_{ij}|y_{ij}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}|^{2}}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})}+\frac{1}{I^{5/2}}\frac{\max_{i}|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}|\max_{ij}|y_{ij}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}|}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})}+\frac{1}{I^{2}}\frac{\max_{i}|\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}|^{2}}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})}

Under the given assumptions, the expression goes to zero.

∎

Lemma C.7
.

\frac{1}{I^{2}J^{2}}\frac{\Var\left[\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma},j\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}}\left(y_{ij}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right)^{2}\right]}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{2}}\xrightarrow{p}0

Proof.

Fix any
\gamma\in\Gamma
and let
d_{ij}=y_{ij}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}
. By the variance formula in
[
23
]
,

\Var\left[\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma},j\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}}d_{ij}(\gamma)^{2}\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}(\{d_{ij}^{2}\})+\frac{1}{J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma}(\{d_{ij}^{2}\})+\frac{1}{IJ}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}(\{d_{ij}^{2}\})

Thus, it suffices to bound
\omega^{\textsf{B}}(d_{ij}^{2})
in terms of
\omega^{\textsf{B}}(y_{ij})
. Observe that

\displaystyle\omega^{\textsf{B}}(d_{ij}^{2})\lesssim\frac{1}{IJ}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\sum_{j=1}^{J}d_{ij}^{4}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{ij}d_{ij}^{2}\frac{1}{IJ}\sum_{ij}d_{ij}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{ij}d_{ij}^{2}\cdot\left[\omega^{\textsf{B}}(y_{ij})+\omega^{\textsf{S}}(y_{ij})+\omega^{\textsf{BS}}(y_{ij})\right].

The same bounds hold for
\omega^{\textsf{S}}(d_{ij}^{2}),\omega^{\textsf{BS}}(d_{ij}^{2})
. Thus,

\frac{1}{I^{2}J^{2}}\frac{\Var\left[\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma},j\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}}\left(y_{ij}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}_{\gamma}\right)^{2}\right]}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{I^{3}}\frac{\max_{ij}|d_{ij}|^{2}}{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})}.

By assumption, the right hand side goes to zero.
∎

Lemma C.8
.

Let
X
be a random variable with finite fourth moment. Set
\mu:=\mathbb{E}X
. Then

\Var(X^{2})\leq\mathbb{E}\left[(X-\mu)^{4}\right]+4|\mu|\mathbb{E}\left[|X-\mu|^{3}\right]+4\mu^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[(X-\mu)^{2}\right].

(81)

Proof.

Set
Y:=X-\mu
. Then
X^{2}=(\mu+Y)^{2}=\mu^{2}+2\mu Y+Y^{2}
and hence

\Var(X^{2})=\Var(2\mu Y+Y^{2})=\Var(Y^{2})+4\mu^{2}\Var(Y)+4\mu\,\Cov(Y,Y^{2}),

Since
\Var(Y)=\mathbb{E}[Y^{2}]
,
\Var(Y^{2})=\mathbb{E}[Y^{4}]-\mathbb{E}[Y^{2}]^{2}
, and
\Cov(Y,Y^{2})=\mathbb{E}[Y^{3}]
:

\Var(X^{2})=\mathbb{E}[(X-\mu)^{4}]-\mathbb{E}[(X-\mu)^{2}]^{2}+4\mu\,\mathbb{E}[(X-\mu)^{3}]+4\mu^{2}\,\mathbb{E}[(X-\mu)^{2}].

Using triangle inequality and removing the negative term gives the result.
∎

Lemma C.9

(ANOVA decomposition for variance)
.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{IJ}\sum_{ij}(y_{ij}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet})^{2}=\omega^{\textsf{BS}}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{IJ}\sum_{ij}(y_{ij}-\overline{y}_{\bullet,j})^{2}=\omega^{\textsf{BS}}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{IJ}\sum_{ij}(y_{ij}-\bar{\bar{y}})^{2}=\omega^{\textsf{BS}}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}

Lemma C.10
.

Recall the notation
d_{ij}:=y_{ij}-\bar{\bar{y}}
. Let
D:=\norm{\left( \eavg{d}_{i,\bullet}\right)_i}=\left(\sum_{i}\hat{\bar{d}}_{i,\bullet}^{2}\right)^{1/2}.
We have
\norm{d_{ij}}_{F}\lesssim I^{2}\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})
and
\mathbb{E}[D^{2}]\lesssim I^{2}\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}).

Proof.

By
Lemma

C.9
,
\norm{d_{ij}}_{F}=\sum_{i,j}(y_{ij}-\bar{\bar{y}})^{2}=\omega^{\textsf{BS}}+\omega^{\textsf{B}}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}
. By
Proposition

A.1
,
\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})\lesssim\frac{1}{I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{1}{J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{1}{IJ}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}
. This shows the first bound. For the second bound, the sampling without replacement variance formula gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[D^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{J^{2}}\sum_{ij}(d_{ij}-\overline{d}_{i,\bullet})^{2}+\sum_{i}\overline{d}_{i,\bullet}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{J^{2}}\sum_{ij}(y_{ij}-\overline{y}_{i,\bullet})^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{I}(\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\omega^{\textsf{BS}}+\omega^{\textsf{S}}+I\omega^{\textsf{B}}

(
Lemma

C.9
)

\displaystyle\lesssim I^{2}\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}).

∎

Lemma C.11

(Trivial Variance Bound)
.

For any two random variables
X,Y
,
\Var(X+Y)\leq 2\Var(X)+2\Var(Y)
. More generally, Let
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
be any collection of random variables. Then

\Var\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\right)\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Var(X_{i}).

Proof.

Cauchy-Schwarz.
∎

Appendix D
Sub-Gaussianity & Moment Bounds

Results in Appendix A of
[
17
]
give us the following.

Lemma D.1

(Sampling without replacement has subgaussian tails)
.

For scalars
u_{1},\ldots,u_{I}
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{i=1}^{I}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}u_{i}-I_{T}\overline{u}\right|>t\right)\leq 2\exp(-\frac{t^2}{8 I \sigma^2_u})

where
\overline{u}=\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i}^{I}u_{i}
and
\sigma^{2}_{u}:=\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}u_{i}^{2}-\left(\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i}u_{i}\right)^{2}
. Thus,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\widehat{\overline{u}}-\overline{u}\right|>t\right)\leq 2\exp(-\frac{t^2}{8 I\sigma^2_u/I_T^2})

where
\widehat{\overline{u}}=\frac{1}{I_{T}}\sum_{i}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}u_{i}
.

By standard formulas,
\Var(\widehat{\overline{u}})=\frac{(I-I_{T})}{I_{T}(I-1)}\sigma_{u}^{2}
. Under the asymptotic scalings for
I,I_{T}
in this paper, we have
I\sigma^{2}_{u}/I_{T}^{2}\leq C\Var(\widehat{\overline{u}})
for some absolute constant
C
. Therefore
\widehat{\overline{u}}
is also subgaussian with parameter
O(\Var(\widehat{\overline{u}}))
.

Lemma D.2

(Vector sampling without replacement has subgaussian tails)
.

Let
u_{1},\dots,u_{I}
now be a finite population of vectors. Then

\mathbb{P}\left(\norm{\sum_i W_i^\tB u_i}_{2}\geq\mathbb{E}\norm{\sum_i W_i^\tB u_i}_{2}+t\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{8\norm{U}_{\operatorname{op}}^{2}}\right)

where the
i
th row of
U
is
u_{i}^{\top}.

See the Proof of Lemma A.3 in
[
17
]
. The other side also holds:

\mathbb{P}\left(\norm{\frac{1}{I_T}\sum_i W_i^\tB u_i}_{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\norm{\frac{1}{I_T}\sum_i W_i^\tB u_i}_{2}-t\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{I_{T}^{2}t^{2}}{8\norm{U}_{\operatorname{op}}^{2}}\right).

Subgaussianity of sampling without replacement gives the following moment bounds.

Lemma D.3
.

For any natural number
k
and scalars
u_{1},\ldots,u_{I}
, such that
\overline{u}=0.

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{I_{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{I}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}u_{i}\right)^{2k}\right]\leq C_{k}\sigma_{u}^{2k}

for some constant
C_{k}
that only depends on
k
, where
\sigma_{u}^{2}
is the finite population variance of the average,

\sigma_{u}^{2}=\left(\frac{1}{I_{T}}-\frac{1}{I}\right)\frac{1}{I-1}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\left(u_{i}-\overline{u}\right)^{2}.

Recall the quantities

\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}}=\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i=1}^{I}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}\delta_{i}^{\textsf{B}}(\gamma),\quad\overline{\epsilon}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{S}}=\frac{1}{J_{\gamma}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}\delta_{j}^{\textsf{S}}(\gamma),\quad\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}}=\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}\sum_{i,j}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}\delta_{ij}^{\textsf{BS}}(\gamma).

(82)

Lemma

A.1
shows that these quantities are uncorrelated.

Lemma D.4

(Even Moment Bound for Decentered Double Average)
.

Let
k
be a nonnegative integer. Then for each
\gamma\in\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}\},

\mathbb{E}\left[(\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma})^{2k}\right]\lesssim C_{k}\Var(\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma})^{k},

for some constant that only depends on
k
.

Proof.

The proof is the same for each
\gamma
so we will ignore dependence on
\gamma.
Write
\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\textsf{BS}}=\frac{1}{I_{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{I}W_{i}^{\textsf{B}}\hat{\bar{\delta}}_{i,\bullet}
where
\hat{\bar{\delta}}_{i,\bullet}=\frac{1}{J_{T}}\sum_{j}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}\delta_{ij}^{\textsf{BS}}
. Conditioning first on
S
, notice that
\sum_{i}\hat{\bar{\delta}}_{i,\bullet}=0
. Using the subgaussian moment bounds for the sample average
\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\textsf{BS}}
from the previous lemma, we find that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\textsf{BS}\ 2k}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C_{k}}{I^{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{I-1}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\hat{\bar{\delta}}_{i,\bullet}^{2}\right)^{k}\right].

Expanding out the
k
th power gives terms of the form
\mathbb{E}\left[\hat{\bar{\delta}}_{i_{1},\bullet}^{2}\dots\hat{\bar{\delta}}_{i_{k},\bullet}^{2}\right]
for possibly non-unique indices
i_{1},\dots,i_{k}
. Using the generalized Holder inequality and sub-Gaussianity of the sample average
\hat{\bar{\delta}}_{i_{j},\bullet}
to bound
\mathbb{E}[\hat{\bar{\delta}}_{i_{j},\bullet}^{2k}]
(using the previous lemma again),

\mathbb{E}\left[\hat{\bar{\delta}}_{i_{1},\bullet}^{2}\dots\hat{\bar{\delta}}_{i_{k},\bullet}^{2}\right]\leq\prod_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\hat{\bar{\delta}}_{i_{j},\bullet}^{2k}\right]^{1/k}\lesssim C_{k}^{k}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\Var(\hat{\bar{\delta}}_{i_{j},\bullet}).

This implies

\mathbb{E}\left[(\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\textsf{BS}})^{2k}\right]\lesssim\frac{C_{k}}{I^{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{I-1}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\Var(\hat{\bar{\delta}}_{i,\bullet})\right]^{k}.

Recall that
\Var(\hat{\bar{\delta}}_{i,\bullet})\lesssim\frac{1}{J^{2}}\sum_{j}(\delta_{ij}^{\textsf{BS}})^{2}.

Thus,
\mathbb{E}\left[(\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\textsf{BS}})^{2k}\right]\leq C_{k}\left(\frac{1}{IJ}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}\right)^{k}
from which the conclusion follows.
∎

Lemma D.5

(4th Moment Bound for Double Average)
.

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right)^{4}\right]\leq C\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}})^{2},

for some constant
C
.

Proof.

Recalling the decomposition
\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}-\bar{\bar{y}}=\overline{\epsilon}^{\textsf{B}}+\overline{\epsilon}^{\textsf{S}}+\bar{\bar{\epsilon}}^{\textsf{BS}}

up to signs, the fourth moment is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\hat{\bar{\bar{y}}}-\bar{\bar{y}}\right)^{4}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b,c\geq 0\\
a+b+c=4\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[(\overline{\epsilon}^{\textsf{B}})^{a}(\overline{\epsilon}^{\textsf{S}})^{b}(\overline{\epsilon}^{\textsf{BS}})^{c}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b,c\geq 0\\
a+b+c=4\end{subarray}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[(\overline{\epsilon}^{\textsf{B}})^{4a}\right]\right)^{1/4}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[(\overline{\epsilon}^{\textsf{S}})^{4b}\right]\right)^{1/4}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[(\overline{\epsilon}^{\textsf{BS}})^{2c}\right]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b,c\geq 0\\
a+b+c=4\end{subarray}}\left(\Var\left[\overline{\epsilon}^{\textsf{B}}\right]\right)^{a/2}\left(\Var\left[\overline{\epsilon}^{\textsf{S}}\right]\right)^{b/2}\left(\Var\left[\overline{\epsilon}^{\textsf{BS}}\right]\right)^{c/2}

\displaystyle=\left(\sqrt{\Var\left[\overline{\epsilon}^{\textsf{B}}\right]}+\sqrt{\Var\left[\overline{\epsilon}^{\textsf{S}}\right]}+\sqrt{\Var\left[\overline{\epsilon}^{\textsf{BS}}\right]}\right)^{4}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\Var\left[\overline{\epsilon}^{\textsf{B}}\right]+\Var\left[\overline{\epsilon}^{\textsf{S}}\right]+\Var\left[\overline{\epsilon}^{\textsf{BS}}\right]\right)^{2},

where we have applied the moment bounds
Lemma

D.3
,
Lemma

D.4
, and Cauchy Schwarz. The last bound follows from Jensen’s inequality.
∎

Appendix E
Proofs for
Section

3

In this section we show the details on proving consistency of the optimal adjustment. As all our results are asymptotic in nature, we will replace all appearances of
I-1,J-1
by
I,J
respectively. For our asymptotic statements, we also interchange
I,J
freely since
I/J\rightarrow\rho\in(0,\infty)
. Throughout this section, define
\hat{e}_{c}
to be
\hat{u}_{c}
replacing the potential outcomes
y_{ij}(\gamma)
by the residuals
e_{ij}(\gamma)=y_{ij}(\gamma)-X_{ij}^{\top}\tilde{\beta}_{c}
, and let
\tilde{e}_{c}
denote the vector
\tilde{u}_{c}
of (
20
) with
y_{ij}
replaced by
e_{ij}
. Further, define
\hat{e}_{\gamma}^{\theta},\tilde{e}_{\gamma}^{\theta}
as the analogues of
\hat{u}_{\gamma}^{\theta},\tilde{u}_{\gamma}^{\theta}
in the same way. We first record some elementary facts that follow from the assumptions of
Section

3
.

Lemma E.1
.

Under
1
and
4
,
\tilde{\beta}_{c}=O(1)
.

Proof.

Write
\tilde{\beta}_{c}=(I\tilde{Z}_{c})^{-1}(I\tilde{u}_{c}).

4
shows
(I\tilde{Z}_{c})^{-1}=O(1)
entrywise while combining
1
with the fact that each
a_{\gamma,\theta}
is
O(1/I)
shows that the entries of
I\tilde{u}_{c}
are
O(1).

∎

Lemma E.2

(Naive Variance Bounds)
.

Under
1
and
2
,
\Var(\hat{\tau}_{c}(\tilde{\beta}_{c}))=O(1/I)
.

Proof.

By inspecting the variance formulas in
Propositions

A.1
and
A.2
, it suffices to prove that
\omega_{\gamma}^{\theta}(e_{ij})=O(1)
and
\xi_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}^{\theta}(e_{ij})=O(1)
for
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}\}
and
\theta\in\{\textsf{B},\textsf{S},\textsf{BS}\}
. Observe the following expansions:

\begin{split}\omega^{\theta}_{\gamma}(e_{ij})&=\beta_{c}^{\top}Z^{\theta}\beta_{c}-2u^{\theta\top}_{\gamma}\beta_{c}+\omega^{\theta}_{\gamma}\\
\xi^{\theta}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(e_{ij})&=\xi^{\theta}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}.\end{split}

(83)

1
and
Lemma

E.1
gives
\omega^{\theta}_{\gamma}(e_{ij})=O(1)
, while Cauchy-Schwarz shows
\xi^{\theta}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}=O(1).

∎

Lemma E.3
.

Under
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
, we have the following bounds on covariate and potential outcome averages:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}|\bar{X}_{i,\bullet}^{(k)}-\bar{\bar{X}}^{(k)}|=o(1/\sqrt{I}),\quad\frac{1}{J}\max_{j}|\bar{X}_{\bullet,j}^{(k)}-\bar{\bar{X}}^{(k)}|=o(1/\sqrt{I})

(84)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I}\max_{ij}|X_{ij}^{(k)}-\bar{X}_{i,\bullet}^{(k)}|=o(1/\sqrt{I}),\quad\frac{1}{I}\max_{ij}|X_{ij}^{(k)}-\bar{X}_{\bullet,j}^{(k)}|=o(1/\sqrt{I}),

(85)

for each coordinate
k=1,\dots,d
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}|\bar{y}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}(\gamma)|=o(1/\sqrt{I}),\quad\frac{1}{I}\max_{j}|\bar{y}_{\bullet,j}(\gamma)-\bar{\bar{y}}(\gamma)|=o(1/\sqrt{I})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I^{3/2}}\max_{i,j}|y_{ij}-\bar{y}_{i,\bullet}|=o(1/\sqrt{I}),\quad\frac{1}{I^{3/2}}\max_{i,j}|y_{ij}-\bar{y}_{\bullet,j}|=o(1/\sqrt{I}).

Proof.

3
immediately gives
M:=\max_{i,j}|X_{ij}^{(k)}-\bar{\bar{X}}^{(k)}|=o(\sqrt{I})
. Since
\max_{i}|\bar{X}_{i,\bullet}^{(k)}-\bar{\bar{X}}^{(k)}|\leq M,\max_{j}|\bar{X}_{\bullet,j}^{(k)}-\bar{\bar{X}}^{(k)}|\leq M
, the triangle inequality establishes (
84
). With
Lemma

E.1
, we conclude

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}|\bar{X}_{i,\bullet}^{\top}\beta_{c}-\bar{\bar{X}}^{\top}\beta_{c}|=o(1/\sqrt{I}),\quad\frac{1}{I}\max_{ij}|X_{ij}^{\top}\beta_{c}-\bar{X}_{\bullet,j}^{\top}\beta_{c}|=o(1/\sqrt{I}),\quad\frac{1}{I}\max_{ij}|X_{ij}^{\top}\beta_{c}-\bar{X}_{i,\bullet}^{\top}\beta_{c}|=o(1/\sqrt{I}).

From
Lemma

E.2
, we know
\Var(\hat{\tau}_{c}(\tilde{\beta}_{c}))=O(1/I).
Combining this fact with
2
gives the second claim.
∎

Lemma E.4

(Decomposition of
\beta_{c}
)
.

\hat{\beta}_{c}-\tilde{\beta}_{c}=\hat{Z}_{c}^{-1}\hat{e}_{c}
. Moreover,
\tilde{Z}_{c}^{-1}\tilde{e}_{c}=0.

Proof.

For the first claim, recall (
24
) and the definitions
\hat{Z}_{c}^{-1}\hat{u}_{c}=\hat{\beta}_{c}
and
\tilde{Z}_{c}^{-1}\tilde{u}_{c}=\tilde{\beta}_{c}
. Then,

\displaystyle\hat{e}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{I-1}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\hat{\bar{X}}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}(\gamma)\hat{\bar{e}}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{I-1}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\hat{\bar{X}}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}(\gamma)\left(\hat{\bar{y}}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}(\gamma)-\hat{\bar{X}}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}(\gamma)^{\top}\tilde{\beta}_{c}\right)

\displaystyle=\hat{u}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}}-\hat{Z}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}}\tilde{\beta}_{c}.

The same argument gives
\hat{e}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{S}}=\hat{u}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{S}}-\hat{Z}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{S}}\tilde{\beta}_{c},\hat{e}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}}=\hat{u}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}}-\hat{Z}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}}\tilde{\beta}_{c}
, from which it follows that
\hat{e}_{c}=\hat{u}_{c}-\hat{Z}_{c}\tilde{\beta}_{c}
.
Left-multiplcation by
\hat{Z}_{c}^{-1}
gives the result. Next, recall (
17
). Replacing
y_{ij}(\gamma)
by
e_{ij}(\gamma)
gives the following:

\displaystyle e_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{I-1}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\bar{X}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}\bar{e}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}(\gamma)=\frac{1}{I-1}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\bar{X}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}(\bar{y}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}(\gamma)-\bar{X}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)\top}\tilde{\beta}_{c})=u_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}}-Z^{\textsf{B}}\tilde{\beta}_{c}.

In the same way,
e_{\gamma}^{\textsf{S}}=u_{\gamma}^{\textsf{S}}-Z^{\textsf{S}}\tilde{\beta}_{c},e_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}}=u_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}}-Z^{\textsf{BS}}\tilde{\beta}_{c}.
As a result,
\tilde{e}_{c}=\tilde{u}_{c}-\tilde{Z}_{c}\tilde{\beta}_{c}
, which gives the second claim.
∎

Firstly, we prove consistency of the estimate for
\beta_{c}
; the result is restated here for convenience.

See
3.1

Proof of
Proposition

3.1
.

Lemma

E.4
allows us to write

\hat{\beta}_{c}-\tilde{\beta}_{c}=\left(\hat{Z}_{c}^{-1}-\tilde{Z}_{c}^{-1}\right)\hat{e}_{c}+\tilde{Z}_{c}^{-1}\left(\hat{e}_{c}-\tilde{e}_{c}\right).

(86)

Then
\tilde{Z}_{c}^{-1}\left(\hat{e}_{c}-\tilde{e}_{c}\right)=(I\tilde{Z}_{c})^{-1}\left(I\hat{e}_{c}-I\tilde{e}_{c}\right)
. Note that
(I\tilde{Z}_{c})^{-1}=O(1)
by
4
and
1
. Next, write

\displaystyle\left(I\hat{e}_{c}-I\tilde{e}_{c}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\gamma}Ia_{\textsf{B},\gamma}(\hat{e}^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}-\tilde{e}^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma})+\sum_{\gamma}Ia_{\textsf{S},\gamma}(\hat{e}^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma}-\tilde{e}^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma})+\sum_{\gamma}Ia_{\textsf{BS},\gamma}(\hat{e}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}-\tilde{e}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}).

By the formulas in
Section

2
,
Ia_{\textsf{B},\gamma}=O(1),Ia_{\textsf{S},\gamma}=O(1)
and
Ia_{\textsf{BS},\gamma}=O(1/I).
To show each of these terms is
o_{p}(1)
, we will first prove that entrywise,
\hat{Z}^{\theta}_{\gamma}-Z^{\theta}=o_{p}(1)
and
\hat{e}^{\theta}_{\gamma}-e^{\theta}_{\gamma}=o_{p}(1)
for all
\theta\in\{\textsf{B},\textsf{S},\textsf{BS}\}
. As the argument will be the same for each
\gamma,
we omit dependence on
\gamma
when clear. We will export this to the next several lemmas. Indeed, apply
Proposition

E.1
with
a_{ij}=X_{ij}^{(k)}
for
k=1,\dots,d
and
b_{ij}=e_{ij}
.
2
implies

\frac{\sqrt{\log I}}{I^{3/2}}\max_{ij}|e_{ij}-\bar{e}_{i,\bullet}|=o\left(\Var(\hat{\tau}_{c}(\tilde{\beta}_{c}))^{1/2}\right),\quad\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}|\bar{e}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{e}}|=o\left(\Var(\hat{\tau}_{c}(\tilde{\beta}_{c}))^{1/2}\right).

By
Lemma

E.2
, the assumptions on
b_{ij}
are satisfied for
Proposition

E.1
. Next,
Lemma

E.3
implies
\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}|\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{a}}|=o(1/\sqrt{I})
, equivalent to
\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}|\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{a}}|^{2}=o(1)
.
Thus
Proposition

E.1
yields
\hat{e}^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}-\tilde{e}^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}=o_{p}(1)
. By symmetry, the same argument holds for the seller terms, so that
\hat{e}^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma}-\tilde{e}^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma}=o_{p}(1)
. Next, applying
Proposition

E.2
with
3
shows that
\hat{e}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}-\tilde{e}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}=o_{p}(1)
. Collecting terms, we have shown
(I\hat{e}_{c}-I\tilde{e}_{c})=o_{p}(1).

For the first term in (
86
), write
\left(\hat{Z}_{c}^{-1}-\tilde{Z}_{c}^{-1}\right)\hat{e}_{c}=\left((I\hat{Z}_{c})^{-1}-(I\tilde{Z}_{c})^{-1}\right)I\hat{e}_{c}
. We have
\norm{I\hat{e}_c}_{2}=O_{p}(1)
since it converges in probability to a vector. To bound the difference of matrix inverses, we will utilize Proposition E.1 of
[
17
]
, which states that for any symmetric matrices
A,B
with
A
positive definite,
A+B
invertible,

\norm{(A+B)^{-1} - A^{-1}}_{\text{op}}\leq\frac{\norm{A^{-1}}_{\text{op}}^{2}\norm{B}_{\text{op}}}{1-\min(1,\norm{A^{-1}}_{\text{op}}\cdot\norm{B}_{\text{op}})}.

Apply this by taking
A:=I\tilde{Z}_{c}
and
B:=I\hat{Z}_{c}-I\tilde{Z}_{c}
. By supposition,
\norm{(I\tilde{Z}_c)^{-1}}_{\text{op}}=O(1)
, so it suffices to show that
\norm{I\hat{Z}_c - I\tilde{Z}_c}_{\text{op}}=o_{p}(1).
We will show that each of the entries of
I\hat{Z}_{c}-I\tilde{Z}_{c}
are
o_{p}(1)
. Using again the formulas in
Section

2
, we have

\displaystyle\left(I\hat{Z}_{c}-I\tilde{Z}_{c}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\gamma}Ia_{\textsf{B},\gamma}(\hat{Z}^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}-\tilde{Z}^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma})+\sum_{\gamma}Ia_{\textsf{S},\gamma}(\hat{Z}^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma}-\tilde{Z}^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma})+\sum_{\gamma}Ia_{\textsf{BS},\gamma}(\hat{Z}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}-\tilde{Z}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}).

Lemma

E.3
shows

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}|\bar{X}_{i,\bullet}^{(k)}-\bar{\bar{X}}^{(k)}|=o(1/\sqrt{I}),\quad\frac{1}{I}\max_{ij}|X_{ij}^{(k)}-\bar{X}_{\bullet,j}^{(k)}|=o(1/\sqrt{I}),\quad\frac{1}{I}\max_{ij}|X_{ij}^{(k)}-\bar{X}_{i,\bullet}^{(k)}|=o(1/\sqrt{I}).

for each coordinate
k
. Applying
Proposition

E.1
with two coordinates of the covariates,
X_{ij}^{(k_{1})},X_{ij}^{(k_{2})}
for
k_{1},k_{2}\in\{1,\dots,d\}
shows
Ia_{\textsf{B},\gamma}(\hat{Z}^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}-\tilde{Z}^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma})
and
Ia_{\textsf{S},\gamma}(\hat{Z}^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma}-\tilde{Z}^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma})
are
o_{p}(1)
. Applying
Proposition

E.2
shows
Ia_{\textsf{BS},\gamma}\left(\hat{Z}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}-\tilde{Z}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}\right)=o_{p}(1).

∎

Proof of
Proposition

3.2
.

We aim to show that

\hat{\tau}_{c}(\hat{\beta}_{c})-\hat{\tau}_{c}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})=o_{p}\left(\sqrt{\Var(\hat{\tau}_{c}(\tilde{\beta}_{c}))}\right).

We may write

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{c}(\hat{\beta}_{c})-\hat{\tau}_{c}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{X}^{\top}(\hat{\beta}_{c}-\tilde{\beta}_{c})

(87)

\displaystyle\mathbb{X}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\gamma}c_{\gamma}\hat{\bar{\bar{X}}}_{\gamma}.

(88)

Since
\sum_{\gamma}c_{\gamma}=0,\mathbb{X}
is mean zero. Thus
\norm{\mathbb{X}}_{2}=O_{p}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{d}\sqrt{\Var(\mathbb{X}^{(k)})}\right)
. By
Proposition

3.1
, it suffices to show that

\Var(\mathbb{X}^{(k)})=O(\Var(\hat{\tau}_{c}(\tilde{\beta}_{c}))),\quad k=1,\dots,d.

By
4
,
\Var(\hat{\tau}_{c}(\tilde{\beta}_{c}))=\Omega(I^{-1})
. It is easy to see that
\omega^{\theta}(\mathbb{X}^{k})=O(1)
for each
\theta\in\{\textsf{B},\textsf{S},\textsf{BS}\}
.
Proposition

A.1
then shows
\Var(\mathbb{X}^{(k)})=O(1/I)
for all
k
, as desired.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

3.2
.

In addition to defining
\hat{V}_{c}(\hat{\beta}_{c})
, define
\hat{V}_{c}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})
to be the conservative variance estimator applied to the matrices
y_{ij}-X_{ij}^{\top}\tilde{\beta}_{c}
and
V_{c}(\hat{\beta}_{c}),V_{c}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})
similarly where
V_{c}
was defined in (
41
). By Slutsky’s theorem and continuous mapping theorem it suffices to show that

\hat{V}_{c}(\hat{\beta}_{c})=(1+o_{p}(1))\hat{V}_{c}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})

By asymptotic consistency of the conservative variance estimator,
\hat{V}_{c}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})-V_{c}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})=o_{p}\left(V_{c}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})\right),
and since the right hand side is a conservative upper bound on the variance, it suffices to show that

\hat{V}_{c}(\hat{\beta}_{c})-\hat{V}_{c}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})=o_{p}\left(\Var(\hat{\tau}_{c}(\tilde{\beta}_{c}))\right)=o_{p}(1/I).

(89)

The last equality follows from
4
.
Let
\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}(\hat{\beta}_{c})
be the analog of (
40
) with outcomes
\hat{e}_{ij}=y_{ij}-X_{ij}\hat{\beta}_{c}
and
\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})
similarly with
\tilde{e}_{ij}.
Because
\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})=O_{p}(1/I),
it is enough to show that
\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}(\hat{\beta}_{c})-\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})=o_{p}(1/I)
. Indeed,

\displaystyle\hat{V}_{c}(\hat{\beta}_{c})

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}|c_{\gamma}|\sqrt{\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}(\hat{\beta}_{c})^{+}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}|c_{\gamma}|\sqrt{\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})^{+}+o_{p}(I^{-1})}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}|c_{\gamma}|\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})^{+}+o_{p}(I^{-1})+2\sum_{\gamma\neq\gamma^{\prime}}|c_{\gamma}||c_{\gamma^{\prime}}|\sqrt{\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})^{+}+o_{p}(I^{-1})}\sqrt{\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})^{+}+o_{p}(I^{-1})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}|c_{\gamma}|\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})^{+}+o_{p}(I^{-1})+2\sum_{\gamma\neq\gamma^{\prime}}|c_{\gamma}||c_{\gamma^{\prime}}|\sqrt{\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})^{+}\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})^{+}}+o_{p}(I^{-1})

\displaystyle=\hat{V}_{c}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})+o_{p}(I^{-1}).

By inspecting the forms of the conservative variance estimators, one can see that

\begin{split}\hat{\omega}^{\theta}_{\gamma}(\hat{\beta}_{c})-\hat{\omega}^{\theta}_{\gamma}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})&=2\hat{u}_{\gamma}^{\theta\top}\left(\hat{\beta}_{c}-\tilde{\beta}_{c}\right)+\left(\hat{\beta}_{c}^{\top}\hat{Z}^{\theta}_{\gamma}\hat{\beta}_{c}-\tilde{\beta}_{c}^{\top}\hat{Z}^{\theta}_{\gamma}\tilde{\beta}_{c}\right)\\
&=2\hat{u}_{\gamma}^{\theta\top}\left(\hat{\beta}_{c}-\tilde{\beta}_{c}\right)+(\hat{\beta}_{c}+\tilde{\beta}_{c})^{\top}\hat{Z}^{\theta}_{\gamma}(\hat{\beta}_{c}-\tilde{\beta}_{c})\end{split}

(90)

for each
\theta\in\{\textsf{B},\textsf{S},\textsf{BS}\},\gamma\in\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}\}.

By
Lemma

E.3
, the assumptions of

Propositions

E.1
and
E.2
are satisfied, when taking
a_{ij}=X^{(k)}_{ij}
and
b_{ij}=y_{ij}(\gamma)
and also when
a_{ij}=X^{(k_{1})}
and
b_{ij}=X^{(k_{2})}_{ij}
. Applying
Proposition

E.1
shows
\hat{u}_{\gamma}^{\theta}-u_{\gamma}^{\theta}=o_{p}(1),\hat{Z}_{\gamma}^{\theta}-Z_{\gamma}^{\theta}=o_{p}(1)
for all
\gamma\in\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}\}
and
\theta\in\{\textsf{B},\textsf{S}\}.
Given that
u_{\gamma}^{\theta},Z_{\gamma}^{\theta}
are
O(1)
from
1
, we conclude that
\hat{u}_{\gamma}^{\theta}=O_{p}(1),\hat{Z}_{\gamma}^{\theta}=O_{p}(1).
Next,
Proposition

E.2
gives
\hat{u}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}},\hat{Z}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}}=O_{p}(1)
and
Proposition

3.1
shows
\hat{\beta}_{c}-\tilde{\beta}_{c}=o_{p}(1)
, so
\hat{\beta}_{c}=O_{p}(1).
Combining these points shows
\hat{\omega}^{\theta}_{\gamma}(\hat{\beta}_{c})-\hat{\omega}^{\theta}_{\gamma}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})=o_{p}(1)
for
\theta\in\{\textsf{B},\textsf{S}\}
and
\hat{\omega}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}(\hat{\beta}_{c})-\hat{\omega}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})=o_{p}(1).

Lastly, consider the second line of (
40
). Simple algebra shows the term can be rewritten

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}\sum_{(i,j)\in\gamma}(y_{ij}(\gamma)-\hat{\bar{y}}_{i,\bullet}(\gamma))^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}\sum_{(i,j)\in\gamma}\hat{y}_{ij}^{(dcr)}(\gamma)^{2}+\frac{1}{J_{\gamma}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}}\hat{\bar{y}}^{(d)}_{\bullet,j}(\gamma)^{2}=\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}}+\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{S}}

Therefore, in the difference
\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}(\hat{\beta}_{c})-\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})
, the second lines contribute at most a constant multiple of

\frac{\alpha^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}}{1-\alpha^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}}\frac{J-J_{\gamma}}{J(J_{\gamma}-1)}\left(\hat{\omega}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}(\hat{\beta}_{c})-\hat{\omega}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})+\hat{\omega}^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma}(\hat{\beta}_{c})-\hat{\omega}^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})\right)

The last term of (
40
) is handled in an analogous way. Recall
\alpha^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma},\alpha^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma}
in (
40
), which are
O(1/I)
. Combining the pieces,

\displaystyle\left|\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}(\hat{\beta}_{c})-\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{I}\left|\hat{\omega}^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}(\hat{\beta}_{c})-\hat{\omega}^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})\right|+\frac{1}{I}\left|\hat{\omega}^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma}(\hat{\beta}_{c})-\hat{\omega}^{\textsf{S}}_{\gamma}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})\right|+\frac{1}{IJ}\left|\hat{\omega}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}(\hat{\beta}_{c})-\hat{\omega}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})\right|,

which shows that
\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}(\hat{\beta}_{c})-\hat{\Sigma}_{\gamma}(\tilde{\beta}_{c})=o_{p}(I^{-1})
as desired.

∎

Proof of
Example

3.1
.

Recall
Example

2.1
shows that
\tilde{Z}_{\dir}=\sum_{\gamma}\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}Z^{\textsf{BS}},\tilde{u}_{\dir}=\sum_{\gamma}\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}u^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}
and
\hat{Z}_{\dir}=\sum_{\gamma}\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}\hat{Z}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma},\hat{u}_{\dir}=\sum_{\gamma}\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}\hat{u}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}
. From
1
it is immediate that
\tilde{\beta}_{\dir}=O(1)
. We will establish the following claims:

1.

The variance of the direct effect estimator is
\Theta(1/IJ)
.

2.

\hat{\beta}_{\dir}-\tilde{\beta}_{\dir}=o_{p}(1)
.

3.

Asymptotic normality of
\hat{\tau}_{\dir}(\hat{\tau}_{\dir})
.

4.

Asymptotically conservative confidence intervals using the plug-in.

For the first claim, it suffices to prove
\omega^{\theta}(e_{ij}(\gamma))=O(1/I)
and also
\xi^{\theta}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}=O(1/I)
for
\theta\in\{\textsf{B},\textsf{S}\}
. Since
\xi^{\theta}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\lesssim\omega^{\theta}_{\gamma}+\omega^{\theta}_{\gamma^{\prime}},
we need only prove
\omega^{\theta}(e_{ij}(\gamma))=O(1/I)
. Expand

\omega^{\theta}(e_{ij}(\gamma))=\beta_{\dir}^{\top}Z^{\theta}\beta_{\dir}-2u^{\top}_{\gamma}\beta_{\dir}+\omega^{\theta}_{\gamma}.

Note that each entry
(k_{1},k_{2})
of
Z^{\theta}
is bounded by a constant multiple of
\omega^{\theta}(X^{(k_{1})})+\omega^{\theta}(X^{(k_{2})})
, which is
O(1/I)
by assumption. Similarly, each coordinate
k
of
u^{\theta}_{\gamma}
is bounded by a constant multiple of
\omega^{\theta}(X^{(k)})+\omega^{\theta}_{\gamma}
, which is also
O(1/I).
The result follows.

Towards the second claim,
Lemma

E.4
gives

\hat{\beta}_{\dir}-\tilde{\beta}_{\dir}=\tilde{Z}_{\dir}^{-1}(\hat{e}_{\dir}-\tilde{e}_{\dir})+(\hat{Z}^{-1}_{\dir}-\tilde{Z}^{-1}_{\dir})\hat{e}_{\dir}.

(91)

Rewrite the first term in (
91
) as
(IJ\tilde{Z}_{\dir})^{-1}IJ(\hat{e}_{\dir}-\tilde{e}_{\dir}).
It is easy to see that
(IJ\tilde{Z}_{\dir})^{-1}=O_{p}(1),
while
IJ(\hat{e}_{\dir}-\tilde{e}_{\dir})=o_{p}(1)
since
\hat{e}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}}-\tilde{e}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}}=o_{p}(1),\forall\gamma\in\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}\}
by
Proposition

E.1
. The second term in (
91
) will be handled in similar fashion as in
Proposition

3.1
.
Write
(\hat{Z}^{-1}_{\dir}-\tilde{Z}^{-1}_{\dir})\hat{e}_{\dir}=\left((IJ\hat{Z}_{\dir})^{-1}-(IJ\tilde{Z}_{\dir})^{-1}\right)IJ\hat{e}_{\dir}
. We have
\norm{IJ\hat{e}_{\dir}}_{2}=O_{p}(1)
combining
1
and
Lemma

E.1
. Apply again Proposition E.1 of
[
17
]
with
A:=IJ\tilde{Z}_{\dir}
and
B:=IJ(\hat{Z}_{\dir}-\tilde{Z}_{\dir})
. By supposition,
\norm{(IJ\tilde{Z}_{\dir})^{-1}}_{\text{op}}=O(1)
, so it suffices to show that
\norm{IJ(\hat{Z}_{\dir} - \tilde{Z}_{\dir})}_{\text{op}}=o_{p}(1).
From the explicit form of the direct effect adjustment, it suffices to show that each entry of
\hat{Z}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}-\tilde{Z}^{\textsf{BS}}
is
o_{p}(1)
. This follows directly from
Proposition

E.2
under
2
and
3
.

Consider now the third claim. Applying
Theorem

3.1
gives

\frac{\hat{\tau}_{\dir}(\tilde{\beta}_{\dir})-\hat{\tau}_{\dir}}{\sqrt{\Var(\hat{\tau}_{\dir}(\tilde{\beta}_{\dir}))}}\Rightarrow N(0,1).

Next, observe

\hat{\tau}_{\dir}(\hat{\beta}_{\dir})-\hat{\tau}_{\dir}(\tilde{\beta}_{\dir})=\left(\hat{\bar{\bar{X}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}-\hat{\bar{\bar{X}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}-\hat{\bar{\bar{X}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}+\hat{\bar{\bar{X}}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}\right)^{\top}(\hat{\beta}_{\dir}-\tilde{\beta}_{\dir}).

The term
\mathbb{X}:=\hat{\bar{\bar{X}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}-\hat{\bar{\bar{X}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}-\hat{\bar{\bar{X}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}+\hat{\bar{\bar{X}}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}
has mean zero. The variance of each coordinate
k
can be read off from
Section

A.1
and is equal to
\sum_{\gamma}\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}(X_{ij}^{(k)})
since the cross-terms
\xi^{\theta}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}
are zero. Under
1
,
\Var(\mathbb{X}^{(k)})=O(1/IJ)
while
\Var(\hat{\tau}_{\dir}(\tilde{\beta}_{\dir}))=\Theta(1/IJ)
. This shows
\hat{\tau}_{\dir}(\hat{\beta}_{\dir})-\hat{\tau}_{\dir}(\tilde{\beta}_{\dir})=o_{p}\left(\sqrt{\Var(\hat{\tau}_{\dir}(\tilde{\beta}_{\dir}))}\right)
, so
\hat{\tau}_{\dir}(\hat{\beta}_{\dir})
has the same asymptotic distribution as that of
\hat{\tau}_{\dir}(\tilde{\beta}_{\dir})
.

For the last claim, the proof of
Theorem

3.2
reduces to showing

\hat{V}_{\dir}(\hat{\beta}_{\dir})-\hat{V}_{\dir}(\tilde{\beta}_{\dir})=o_{p}(1/IJ)=o_{p}\left(\Var(\hat{\tau}_{\dir}(\tilde{\beta}_{\dir}))\right).

Recalling (
90
), it suffices to show that
\hat{u}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma},\hat{Z}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}=O_{p}(1)
and
\hat{u}^{\theta}_{\gamma},\hat{Z}^{\theta}_{\gamma}=O_{p}(1/I)
for
\theta\in\{\textsf{B},\textsf{S}\}
. The former claim is immediate from
Proposition

E.2
and
u^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma},Z^{\textsf{BS}}_{\gamma}=O_{p}(1)
via
1
. For the latter, we adapt the proof of
Proposition

E.1
to show that
\hat{u}^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}-\tilde{u}^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}=O_{p}(1/I)
,
\hat{Z}^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}-\tilde{Z}^{\textsf{B}}=O_{p}(1/I)
, and similarly for the seller terms. Fix a coordinate
k
and omit the dependence on
\gamma
. Then
Proposition

E.1
stochastically bounds the
k
th coordinate of the difference
\hat{u}^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}-\tilde{u}^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}
by

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{J}+\left(\frac{1}{I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}(X^{(k)})\right)^{1/2}+\left(\frac{1}{I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}(y)\right)^{1/2}\\
+&\left(\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}(\bar{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}})^{2}\omega^{\textsf{B}}(X^{(k)})\right)^{1/2}.\end{split}

(92)

We will show each of these terms are
O(1/I)
. To do so, the analog of
Lemma

E.3
is needed in this setting. First, we bound

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}|\bar{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}}|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}|\bar{e}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{e}}|+\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}|\bar{X}_{i,\bullet}^{\top}\tilde{\beta}_{\dir}-\bar{\bar{X}}^{\top}\tilde{\beta}_{\dir}|

\displaystyle=o(\sqrt{\Var(\hat{\tau}_{\dir})})+\frac{1}{I}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\max_{i}|\bar{X}_{i,\bullet}^{(k)}-\bar{\bar{X}}^{(k)}||\tilde{\beta}_{\dir}^{(k)}|

\displaystyle=O(1/I),

since
\max_{i}|\bar{X}_{i,\bullet}^{(k)}-\bar{\bar{X}}^{(k)}|=O(1)
. By the assumptions of
Example

3.1
,
\omega^{\textsf{B}}(X^{(k)})=O(1/I),

\max_{i}|\bar{X}_{i,\bullet}^{(k)}-\bar{\bar{X}}^{(k)}|=O(1)
and
\omega^{\textsf{B}}(y)=O(1/I).
This shows that every term in the first line of (
92
) is
O(1/I).
For the last term,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}(\bar{y}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{y}})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}(\bar{e}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{e}})^{2}+\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}|\bar{X}_{i,\bullet}^{\top}\tilde{\beta}_{\dir}-\bar{\bar{X}}^{\top}\tilde{\beta}_{\dir}|^{2}

\displaystyle=o(I\Var(\hat{\tau}_{\dir}))+O(1/I)

\displaystyle=O(1/I).

The analysis of
\hat{Z}^{\textsf{B}}_{\gamma}-\tilde{Z}^{\textsf{B}}
is done the same way, applying
Proposition

E.1
entrywise with two coordinates of the covariate vector. ∎

E.1
Auxiliary Results

Proposition E.1
.

Let
(a_{ij}),(b_{ij})
be a sequence of
I\times J
matrices, indexed by
n
. Suppose that the limits of
\omega^{\theta}(a)
and
\omega^{\theta}(b),\theta\in\{\textsf{B},\textsf{S},\textsf{BS}\}
exist and are finite, where
\omega^{\theta}(-)
is defined in (
13
). Suppose either
\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}|\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{a}}|^{2}=o(1)
or
\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}|\bar{b}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{b}}|^{2}=o(1)
. Then

\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}\hat{\bar{a}}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}\hat{\bar{b}}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}-\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\bar{a}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}\bar{b}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}=o_{p}(1).

(93)

By symmetry, the analogous result is true when randomizing over the sellers.

Proof.

Combining the definition of
\hat{\bar{a}}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet},\,\hat{\bar{b}}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}
with a simple manipulation gives

\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}\hat{\bar{a}}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}\hat{\bar{b}}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}=\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}(\hat{\bar{a}}_{i,\bullet}-\hat{\bar{\bar{a}}})(\hat{\bar{b}}_{i,\bullet}-\hat{\bar{\bar{b}}})=\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}(\hat{\bar{a}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{a}})(\hat{\bar{b}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{b}})-(\hat{\bar{\bar{a}}}-\bar{\bar{a}})(\hat{\bar{\bar{b}}}-\bar{\bar{b}}).

We have
(\hat{\bar{\bar{a}}}-\bar{\bar{a}})(\hat{\bar{\bar{b}}}-\bar{\bar{b}})=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{a}}})\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{b}}})}\right)
, by Cauchy-Schwarz and Chebyshev. Under the assumption that
\omega^{\theta}(a)=O(1)
and
\omega^{\theta}(b)=O(1)
for all
\theta\in\textsf{B},\textsf{S},\textsf{BS}
, we have
\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{a}}})=O(1/I)
and similarly with
\Var(\hat{\bar{\bar{b}}})
by Proposition
A.1
. Thus

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}\hat{\bar{a}}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}\hat{\bar{b}}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}-\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\bar{a}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}\bar{b}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}(\hat{\bar{a}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{a}})(\hat{\bar{b}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{b}})-\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\bar{a}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}\bar{b}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}+O_{p}(1/I)

Decompose the quantity on the right hand side as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}(\hat{\bar{a}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{a}_{i,\bullet})(\hat{\bar{b}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})

(94)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}(\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{a}})(\hat{\bar{b}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})

(95)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}(\hat{\bar{a}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{a}_{i,\bullet})(\bar{b}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{b}})

(96)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}(\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{a}})(\bar{b}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{b}})-\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}(\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{a}})(\bar{b}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{b}})

(97)

1.

For the first term (
94
), the mean is given by
\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\Cov(\hat{\bar{a}}_{i,\bullet},\hat{\bar{b}}_{i,\bullet})
using the tower property. Using the covariance formula for sampling without replacement, this is upper bounded by a constant multiple times

\frac{1}{IJ^{2}}\sum_{i,j}(a_{ij}-\bar{a}_{i,\bullet})(b_{ij}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet}).

Applying Cauchy-Schwarz gives the bound

\frac{1}{J}\left(\frac{1}{IJ}\sum_{i,j}(a_{ij}-\bar{a}_{i,\bullet})^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{IJ}\sum_{i,j}(b_{ij}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})^{2}\right)^{1/2}=\frac{1}{J}\left((\omega^{\textsf{BS}}(a)+\omega^{\textsf{S}}(a))(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}(b)+\omega^{\textsf{S}}(b))\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

which is
O(1/J)
by assumption. Let us now inspect the variance of the term. We apply the law of total variance, conditioning on the buyer indicator variables, to obtain

\Var\left(\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}\Cov(\hat{\bar{a}}_{i,\bullet},\hat{\bar{b}}_{i,\bullet})\right)+\mathbb{E}\left[\Var\left(\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}(\hat{\bar{a}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{a}_{i,\bullet})(\hat{\bar{b}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})\middle|\textsf{B}\right)\right]

(98)

Firstly,

\Var\left(\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}\Cov(\hat{\bar{a}}_{i,\bullet},\hat{\bar{b}}_{i,\bullet})\right)\lesssim\frac{1}{I^{2}}\sum_{i}\Cov(\hat{\bar{a}}_{i,\bullet},\hat{\bar{b}}_{i,\bullet})^{2}\leq\left(\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i}|\Cov(\hat{\bar{a}}_{i,\bullet},\hat{\bar{b}}_{i,\bullet})|\right)^{2}=O(1/J^{2}).

To handle the second term in (
98
), define the vectors
u_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{I_{T}},j=1,\dots,J
with entries
a_{ij}-\bar{a}_{i,\bullet},i\in\mathcal{I}_{T}
and
v_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{I_{T}}
with entries
b_{ij}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet},i\in\mathcal{I}_{T}
. Then

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}(\hat{\bar{a}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{a}_{i,\bullet})(\hat{\bar{b}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})=\frac{1}{J_{T}^{2}}\langle\sum_{j}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}u_{j},\sum_{j}W_{j}^{\textsf{S}}v_{j}\rangle=\frac{1}{J_{T}^{2}}W^{\textsf{S}\top}AW^{\textsf{S}}

for a matrix
A
with entries
A_{ij}=\langle u_{i},v_{j}\rangle.
Notice that
\sum_{j}u_{j}=0,\sum_{j}v_{j}=0
so the row and column sums of
A_{ij}
are zero. Applying Lemma A.5 of
[
17
]
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\Var\left(\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}(\hat{\bar{a}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{a}_{i,\bullet})(\hat{\bar{b}}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})|\textsf{B}\right)\right]\leq\frac{1}{I_{T}^{2}J_{T}^{4}}\norm{A}_{F}^{2}=\frac{1}{J_{T}^{4}}\sum_{j,k}\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{1}{I_{T}}u_{j}^{\top}v_{k}\right)^{2}

Since
u_{j}^{\top}v_{k}=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{T}}(a_{ij}-\bar{a}_{i,\bullet})(b_{ik}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})
, we can use the mean and variance formulas for sampling without replacement to obtain

\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{1}{I_{T}}u_{j}^{\top}v_{k}\right)^{2}\lesssim\left(\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}(a_{ij}-\bar{a}_{i,\bullet})(b_{ik}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{I^{2}}\sum_{i=1}(a_{ij}-\bar{a}_{i,\bullet})^{2}(b_{ik}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})^{2}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{J_{T}^{4}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{J}\left(\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}(a_{ij}-\bar{a}_{i,\bullet})(b_{ik}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{J_{T}^{4}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{J}\frac{1}{I^{2}}\sum_{i=1}(a_{ij}-\bar{a}_{i,\bullet})^{2}(b_{ik}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{J_{T}^{4}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{J}\left(\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}(a_{ij}-\bar{a}_{i,\bullet})(b_{ik}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{J_{T}^{4}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{J}\left(\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}|(a_{ij}-\bar{a}_{i,\bullet})(b_{ik}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})|\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{J_{T}^{4}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{J}\left(\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}(a_{ij}-\bar{a}_{i,\bullet})^{2}\right)\left(\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}(b_{ik}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{J^{2}}(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}(a)+\omega^{\textsf{S}}(a))(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}(b)+\omega^{\textsf{S}}(b)).

The total contribution for the first term is thus
O(1/J)
.

2.

For the second term (
95
),
conditioning on
B
shows that the expression is mean zero. Thus it suffices to compute the variance. Using the law of total variance and conditioning on the buyer variables, the variance is given by

\mathbb{E}\left[\Var\left[\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}\bar{a}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}\hat{\bar{b}}_{i,\bullet}\ |\ \textsf{B}\right]\right].

The inner term is the finite sample variance of the population indexed by
j
given by
\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}(\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{a}})b_{ij}
, yielding

\displaystyle\frac{1}{J^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}\bar{a}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}(b_{ij}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{J^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left[\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\bar{a}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}(b_{ij}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{I^{2}J^{2}}\sum_{i,j}^{I,J}\bar{a}^{(d)2}_{i,\bullet}(b_{ij}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})^{2}-\frac{1}{IJ^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left(\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}(b_{ij}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{J^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left[\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\bar{a}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}(b_{ij}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})\right]^{2}+\frac{1}{I^{2}J^{2}}\sum_{i,j}^{I,J}\bar{a}^{(d)2}_{i,\bullet}(b_{ij}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{J^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left[\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\left|\bar{a}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}(b_{ij}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})\right|\right]^{2}.

Applying Cauchy Schwarz,

\frac{1}{J^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left[\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\left|(\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{a}})(b_{ij}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})\right|\right]^{2}\leq\frac{1}{IJ^{2}}\sum_{i,j}^{I,J}(b_{ij}-\bar{b}_{i,\bullet})^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}(\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{a}})^{2}

In turn, bound this by

\frac{1}{J}\left(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}(b)+\omega^{\textsf{S}}(b)\right)\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}(\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{a}})^{2}=\frac{1}{I}\left(\omega^{\textsf{BS}}(b)+\omega^{\textsf{S}}(b)\right)\omega^{\textsf{B}}(a)=O\left(\frac{1}{I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}(a)\right)

Thus the total contribution from the second term is
O_{p}\left(\left(\frac{1}{I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}(a)\right)^{1/2}\right)
.

3.

The third term is symmetric to the second term and is
O_{p}\left(\left(\frac{1}{I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}(b)\right)^{1/2}\right)
.

4.

For the last term, bound it above by
O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\Var(\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}\bar{a}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}\bar{b}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet})}\right)
. Note that

\displaystyle\Var\left(\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}\bar{a}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}\bar{b}^{(d)}_{i,\bullet}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{I^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{I}(\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{a}})^{2}(\bar{b}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{b}})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}(\bar{b}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{b}})^{2}\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}(\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{a}})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}(\bar{b}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{b}})^{2}\omega^{\textsf{B}}(a).

We could have equivalently used the bound
\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}(\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{a}})^{2}\omega^{\textsf{B}}(b)
for the last term.

We have thus shown the left hand side of (
93
) is stochastically bounded by

\frac{1}{J}+\left(\frac{1}{I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}(a)\right)^{1/2}+\left(\frac{1}{I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}(b)\right)^{1/2}+\left(\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}(\bar{b}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{b}})^{2}\omega^{\textsf{B}}(a)\wedge\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}(\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{a}})^{2}\omega^{\textsf{B}}(b)\right)^{1/2}

By assumption,
\omega^{\textsf{B}}(a),\omega^{\textsf{B}}(b)=O(1)
. Under the condition that either
\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}(\bar{b}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{b}})^{2}=o(1)
or
\frac{1}{I}\max_{i}(\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}-\bar{\bar{a}})^{2}=o(1)
, the result follows.
∎

Proposition E.2
.

Let
(a_{ij}),(b_{ij})
be a sequence of
I\times J
matrices, indexed by
n
. Suppose the same conditions of
Proposition

E.1
and further condition that either
\frac{1}{I}\max_{ij}|a_{ij}-\bar{\bar{a}}|^{2}=o(1)
or
\frac{1}{I}\max_{ij}|b_{ij}-\bar{\bar{b}}|^{2}=o(1)
. Then

\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}\sum_{(i,j)\in\gamma}\hat{a}^{(dcr)}_{ij}\hat{b}^{(dcr)}_{ij}-\frac{1}{IJ}\sum_{i,j}a^{(dcr)}_{ij}b^{(dcr)}_{ij}=o_{p}(1).

(99)

Proof.

Since double decentering forces each row and column sum to be zero, it follows that
\sum_{(i,j)\in\gamma}\hat{a}^{(dcr)}_{ij}\hat{b}^{(dcr)}_{ij}=\sum_{(i,j)\in\gamma}a_{ij}\hat{b}^{(dcr)}_{ij}
and similarly
\sum_{i,j}a_{ij}^{(dcr)}b^{(dcr)}_{ij}=\sum_{i,j}a_{ij}b^{(dcr)}_{ij}.
From this, one can show that the left hand side of (
99
) is equal to

\displaystyle\widehat{\bar{\bar{ab}}}-\bar{\bar{ab}}

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}\hat{\bar{a}}_{i,\bullet}\hat{\bar{b}}_{i,\bullet}-\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}\bar{b}_{i,\bullet}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{J_{\gamma}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}}\hat{\bar{a}}_{j,\bullet}\hat{\bar{b}}_{i,\bullet}-\frac{1}{J}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\bar{a}_{j,\bullet}\bar{b}_{j,\bullet}\right)+\left(\hat{\bar{\bar{a}}}\hat{\bar{\bar{b}}}-\bar{\bar{a}}\bar{\bar{b}}\right).

Now, observe that

\displaystyle\widehat{\bar{\bar{ab}}}-\bar{\bar{ab}}=\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}\sum_{i,j\in\gamma}(a_{ij}-\bar{\bar{a}})(b_{ij}-\bar{\bar{b}})-\frac{1}{IJ}\sum_{ij}(a_{ij}-\bar{\bar{a}})(b_{ij}-\bar{\bar{b}})+(\bar{\bar{a}}\hat{\bar{\bar{b}}}+\hat{\bar{\bar{a}}}\bar{\bar{b}}-2\bar{\bar{a}}\bar{\bar{b}}),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}\hat{\bar{a}}_{i,\bullet}\hat{\bar{b}}_{i,\bullet}-\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}\bar{b}_{i,\bullet}=\left(\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}\hat{\bar{a}}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}\hat{\bar{b}}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}-\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}\bar{b}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}\right)+(\hat{\bar{\bar{a}}}\hat{\bar{\bar{b}}}-\bar{\bar{a}}\bar{\bar{b}})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{J_{\gamma}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}}\hat{\bar{a}}_{\bullet,j}\hat{\bar{b}}_{\bullet,j}-\frac{1}{J}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\bar{a}_{\bullet,j}\bar{b}_{\bullet,j}=\left(\frac{1}{J_{\gamma}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}}\hat{\bar{a}}_{\bullet,j}^{(d)}\hat{\bar{b}}_{\bullet,j}^{(d)}-\frac{1}{J}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\bar{a}_{\bullet,j}^{(d)}\bar{b}_{\bullet,j}^{(d)}\right)+(\hat{\bar{\bar{a}}}\hat{\bar{\bar{b}}}-\bar{\bar{a}}\bar{\bar{b}}).

Thus, the left hand side of (
99
) can equivalently be written as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}\sum_{i,j\in\gamma}(a_{ij}-\bar{\bar{a}})(b_{ij}-\bar{\bar{b}})-\frac{1}{IJ}\sum_{ij}(a_{ij}-\bar{\bar{a}})(b_{ij}-\bar{\bar{b}})

(100)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}\hat{\bar{a}}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}\hat{\bar{b}}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}-\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\bar{a}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}\bar{b}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}\right)

(101)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{J_{\gamma}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}}\hat{\bar{a}}_{\bullet,j}^{(d)}\hat{\bar{b}}_{\bullet,j}^{(d)}-\frac{1}{J}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\bar{a}_{\bullet,j}^{(d)}\bar{b}_{\bullet,j}^{(d)}\right)

(102)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(\hat{\bar{\bar{a}}}-\bar{\bar{a}})(\hat{\bar{\bar{b}}}-\bar{\bar{b}}).

(103)

By the same argument as
Proposition

E.1
the last three terms are
o_{p}(1)
. For the first term (
100
), we apply
Lemma

E.5
. Here, note by Jensen that

\frac{1}{I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}(a^{(d)}b^{(d)})\lesssim\frac{1}{I^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\left(\frac{1}{J}\sum_{j=1}^{J}a_{ij}^{(d)}b_{ij}^{(d)}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{1}{I}\max_{ij}(a_{ij}-\bar{\bar{a}})^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{IJ}\sum_{ij}(b_{ij}-\bar{\bar{b}})^{2}.

(104)

Under the assumed condition that
\frac{1}{I}\max_{ij}|a_{ij}-\bar{\bar{a}}|^{2}=o(1)
, (
104
) is
o(1)
. By symmetry a similar bound holds for
\frac{1}{J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}(a^{(d)}b^{(d)}).
Lastly, by the same argument as in the beginning of the proof,
\omega^{\textsf{BS}}(y)=\frac{1}{IJ}\sum_{ij}^{I,J}y_{ij}^{2}-\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\bar{y}_{i,\bullet}^{2}-\frac{1}{J}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\bar{y}_{\bullet,j}^{2}+\bar{\bar{y}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{IJ}\sum_{ij}^{I,J}y_{ij}^{2}+\bar{\bar{y}}^{2}
. Combining this with Jensen’s inequality,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{IJ}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}(a^{(d)}b^{(d)})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{I^{2}J^{2}}\sum_{i,j}(a_{ij}-\bar{\bar{a}})^{2}(b_{ij}-\bar{\bar{b}})^{2}+\frac{1}{IJ}\left(\frac{1}{IJ}\sum_{i,j}(a_{ij}-\bar{\bar{a}})(b_{ij}-\bar{\bar{b}})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{I^{2}J^{2}}\sum_{i,j}(a_{ij}-\bar{\bar{a}})^{2}(b_{ij}-\bar{\bar{b}})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{I^{2}}\max_{i,j}(a_{ij}-\bar{\bar{a}})^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{IJ}\sum_{i,j}(b_{ij}-\bar{\bar{b}})^{2}=o(1).

(
100
) could have been equivalently bounded by switching
a
and
b
, so it is also
o_{p}(1)
if
\frac{1}{I}\max_{ij}\left|b_{ij}-\bar{\bar{b}}\right|^{2}=o_{p}(1).
Collecting the terms shows that (
100
) is
o_{p}(1)
.

∎

Lemma E.5
.

Under the same assumptions as above, we have

\hat{\bar{\bar{ab}}}-\bar{\bar{ab}}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{I}\omega^{\textsf{B}}(ab)+\frac{1}{J}\omega^{\textsf{S}}(ab)+\frac{1}{IJ}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}(ab)}\right).

Proof.

Simply apply the variance formula in
Appendix

A
.
∎

Appendix F
Optimal Interacted Regression Adjustments

In this section, we consider regression adjustments of the form

\hat{\tau}_{c,\text{int}}(\bm{\beta}):=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}c_{\gamma}\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}J_{\gamma}}\sum_{(i,j)\in\gamma}y_{ij}-\beta_{\gamma}^{\top}X_{ij}.

We will refer to these as
interacted imputation estimators
.
For the purposes of not further complicating the paper, we only derive the forms of optimal regression adjustments without further theoretical analysis.
Our techniques for the non-interacted setting developed in
Appendix

E
should apply here.

Let us recall the notation

u_{\gamma}^{\textsf{B}}:=\frac{1}{I-1}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\overline{X}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}\overline{y}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}(\gamma),\quad u_{\gamma}^{\textsf{S}}=\frac{1}{J-1}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\overline{X}_{\bullet,j}^{(d)}\overline{y}_{\bullet,j}^{(d)}(\gamma),\quad u_{\gamma}^{\textsf{BS}}=\frac{1}{(I-1)(J-1)}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\sum_{j=1}^{J}X_{ij}^{(dcr)}y_{ij}^{(dcr)}(\gamma).

and where
Z
is a known linear combination of the matrices

Z^{\textsf{B}}:=\frac{1}{I}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\overline{X}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}\overline{X}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)\top},\quad Z^{\textsf{S}}:=\frac{1}{J}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\overline{X}_{\bullet,j}^{(d)}\overline{X}_{\bullet,j}^{(d)\top},\quad Z^{\textsf{BS}}:=\frac{1}{IJ}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\sum_{j=1}^{J}X_{ij}^{(dcr)}X_{ij}^{(dcr)\top}.

Expanding,

\begin{split}\omega^{\theta}_{\gamma}(e_{ij})&:=\beta_{\gamma}^{\top}Z^{\theta}\beta_{\gamma}-2u^{\theta\top}_{\gamma}\beta_{\gamma}+\omega^{\theta}_{\gamma}\\
\xi^{\theta}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}(e_{ij})&:=(\beta_{\gamma}-\beta_{\gamma}^{\prime})^{\top}Z^{\theta}(\beta_{\gamma}-\beta_{\gamma}^{\prime})-2(u^{\theta}_{\gamma}-u^{\theta}_{\gamma^{\prime}})^{\top}(\beta_{\gamma}-\beta_{\gamma^{\prime}})+\xi^{\theta}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\\
&=\beta_{\gamma}^{\top}Z^{\theta}\beta_{\gamma}-2\beta_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{\top}Z^{\theta}\beta_{\gamma}+\beta_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{\top}Z^{\theta}\beta_{\gamma^{\prime}}\\
&-2\left(u_{\gamma}^{\theta\top}\beta_{\gamma}+u_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{\theta\top}\beta_{\gamma^{\prime}}-u_{\gamma}^{\theta\top}\beta_{\gamma^{\prime}}-u_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{\theta\top}\beta_{\gamma}\right)+\xi^{\theta}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\end{split}

(105)

Let
\bm{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}^{4d}
be the concatenation of the adjustments
\beta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}},\beta_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}},\beta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}},\beta_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}.
We can thus see
\Var(\hat{\tau}_{c,\text{int}}(\bm{\beta}))
is a quadratic form in
\bm{\beta}
which can be written as a block equation

\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{\beta}-2\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\beta}+\Var(\hat{\tau}_{c}),

with
\bm{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{4d\times 4d},\bm{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{4d}.
Note that
\bm{Z}
only depends on the covariates while
\bm{u}
only depends on potential outcome contrasts of the form
u^{\theta}_{\gamma}-u^{\theta}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
which are estimable via the formulas in
Section

2
. Thus, the optimal regression-adjusted coefficient is estimable from data. The next several subsections detail the explicit forms of the adjustment for the direct, total, and spillover effects, alongside natural estimators.

The resulting estimators are generally different from running interacted least squares (taking
\beta_{\gamma}
to be the OLS coefficient in each group), which we will call the Lin estimator
[
19
]
.
Figure

6
gives a simulation setting where the optimal interacted adjustment for the total effect is considerably more efficient than the Lin procedure. In other settings, we find that the optimal interacted procedure and the Lin estimator are comparable; the Lin estimator has slightly better small sample performance, especially when one of the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}
blocks is small. We leave a detailed numerical and theoretical study to future work. The tools developed in this paper should be enough to prove the analogous results in
Section

3
.

Figure 6
:
KDE estimate of sampling distributions of the Lin estimator, optimal interacted adjustment, ANCOVA, and unadjusted estimators in a synthetic setting, where the treatment indicators were rerandomized over 2000 Monte Carlo runs. The potential outcomes were taken as
y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})=\mu^{\textsf{B}}_{i}+\mu^{\textsf{S}}_{j}+\epsilon_{ij}
, where each of
\mu_{i}^{\textsf{B}},\mu^{\textsf{S}}_{j},\epsilon_{ij}
are independent standard normals and
y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}})=y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})+5.

X_{ij}\in\mathbb{R}
is taken to be a noisy observation of the control potential outcome
y_{ij}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})
. The relative efficiency of the optimal interacted estimator with respect to the Lin estimator is
\sim 0.3
.

F.1
Interacted Estimators for the Direct Effect

Recall that the variance expression for the direct effect estimator can be written in (
26
):

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I_{T}J_{T}}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}+\frac{1}{I_{T}J_{C}}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}+\frac{1}{I_{C}J_{T}}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}+\frac{1}{I_{C}J_{C}}\omega^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}

\displaystyle+\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}-\frac{1}{I}\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}+\frac{1}{I}\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}+\frac{1}{I}\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\frac{1}{I}\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}}\frac{1}{I}\xi^{\textsf{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{J}\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}}\frac{1}{J}\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}+\frac{1}{J}\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}+\frac{1}{J}\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}}\frac{1}{J}\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\frac{1}{J}\xi^{\textsf{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle-\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}}\frac{1}{IJ}\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}-\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}}\frac{1}{IJ}\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\frac{1}{IJ}\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\frac{1}{IJ}\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}-\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}}\frac{1}{IJ}\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}-\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}}\frac{1}{IJ}\xi^{\textsf{BS}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}.

We record the formula for
\bm{Z}_{\dir}:=\bm{Z}
here, as a
4\times 4
block matrix with block entries
\bm{Z}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}
.
By symmetry,
\bm{Z}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}=\bm{Z}_{\gamma^{\prime},\gamma}
so we only record the upper diagonal block entries.

\displaystyle\bm{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{1}{I_{T}J_{T}}-\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}IJ}-\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}IJ}-\frac{1}{IJ}\right)Z^{\textsf{BS}}+\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}Z^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}Z^{\textsf{S}}

\displaystyle\bm{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}Z^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{1}{J}Z^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}}\frac{1}{IJ}Z^{\textsf{BS}}

\displaystyle\bm{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{I}Z^{\textsf{B}}-\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}}\frac{1}{J}Z^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}}\frac{1}{IJ}Z^{\textsf{BS}}

\displaystyle\bm{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{I}Z^{\textsf{B}}-\frac{1}{J}Z^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{1}{IJ}Z^{\textsf{BS}}

\displaystyle\bm{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{1}{I_{T}J_{C}}-\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}IJ}-\frac{1}{IJ}-\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}IJ}\right)Z^{\textsf{BS}}+\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}Z^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}Z^{\textsf{S}}

\displaystyle\bm{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{I}Z^{\textsf{B}}-\frac{1}{J}Z^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{1}{IJ}Z^{\textsf{BS}}

\displaystyle\bm{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{I}Z^{\textsf{B}}-\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}}\frac{1}{J}Z^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}}\frac{1}{IJ}Z^{\textsf{BS}}

\displaystyle\bm{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{1}{I_{C}J_{T}}-\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}IJ}-\frac{1}{IJ}-\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}IJ}\right)Z^{\textsf{BS}}+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}Z^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}Z^{\textsf{S}}

\displaystyle\bm{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}Z^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{1}{J}Z^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}}\frac{1}{IJ}Z^{\textsf{BS}}

\displaystyle\bm{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{1}{I_{C}J_{C}}-\frac{1}{IJ}-\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}IJ}-\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}IJ}\right)Z^{\textsf{BS}}+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}Z^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}Z^{\textsf{S}}.

Simplifying the diagonal terms slightly,

\displaystyle Z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{I_{C}J_{C}}{I_{T}J_{T}}\frac{1}{IJ}Z^{\textsf{BS}}+\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}Z^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}Z^{\textsf{S}}

\displaystyle Z_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}},{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{I_{C}J_{T}}{I_{T}J_{C}}\frac{1}{IJ}Z^{\textsf{BS}}+\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}Z^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}Z^{\textsf{S}}

\displaystyle Z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{I_{T}J_{C}}{I_{C}J_{T}}\frac{1}{IJ}Z^{\textsf{BS}}+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}Z^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}Z^{\textsf{S}}

\displaystyle Z_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{I_{T}J_{T}}{I_{C}J_{C}}\frac{1}{IJ}Z^{\textsf{BS}}+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}Z^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}Z^{\textsf{S}}.

Now, we record the block formula for
\bm{u}
where the block entries
\bm{u}_{\gamma}
are the terms multiplied by
\beta_{\gamma}
.

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{I_{T}J_{T}}u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{BS}}-\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}IJ}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{BS}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}^{\textsf{BS}})-\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}IJ}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{BS}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}^{\textsf{BS}})-\frac{1}{IJ}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{BS}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{BS}})

\displaystyle+\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{B}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}^{\textsf{B}})-\frac{1}{I}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{B}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}^{\textsf{B}})+\frac{1}{I}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{B}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{B}})

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{J}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{S}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}^{\textsf{S}})+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{S}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}^{\textsf{S}})+\frac{1}{J}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{S}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{S}})

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{I_{T}J_{C}}u_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}^{\textsf{BS}}-\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}IJ}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}^{\textsf{BS}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{BS}})-\frac{1}{IJ}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}^{\textsf{BS}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}^{\textsf{BS}})-\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}IJ}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}^{\textsf{BS}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{BS}})

\displaystyle+\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}^{\textsf{B}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{B}})+\frac{1}{I}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}^{\textsf{B}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}^{\textsf{B}})-\frac{1}{I}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}^{\textsf{B}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{B}})

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{J}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}^{\textsf{S}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{S}})+\frac{1}{J}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}^{\textsf{S}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}^{\textsf{S}})+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}^{\textsf{S}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{S}})

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{I_{C}J_{T}}u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}^{\textsf{BS}}-\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}IJ}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}^{\textsf{BS}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{BS}})-\frac{1}{IJ}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}^{\textsf{BS}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}^{\textsf{BS}})-\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}IJ}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}^{\textsf{BS}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{BS}})

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{I}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}^{\textsf{B}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{B}})+\frac{1}{I}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}^{\textsf{B}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}^{\textsf{B}})+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}^{\textsf{B}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{B}})

\displaystyle+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}^{\textsf{S}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{S}})+\frac{1}{J}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}^{\textsf{S}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}^{\textsf{S}})-\frac{1}{J}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}^{\textsf{S}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{S}})

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{I_{C}J_{C}}u_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{BS}}-\frac{1}{IJ}(u_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{BS}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{BS}})-\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}IJ}(u_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{BS}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}^{\textsf{BS}})-\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}IJ}(u_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{BS}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}^{\textsf{BS}})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{I}(u_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{B}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{B}})-\frac{1}{I}(u_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{B}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}^{\textsf{B}})+\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}(u_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{B}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}^{\textsf{B}})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{J}(u_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{S}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{S}})+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}(u_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{S}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\operatorname{ib}}}^{\textsf{S}})-\frac{1}{J}(u_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{S}}-u_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{is}}}^{\textsf{S}}).

The optimal adjustment is then given by
\tilde{\bm{\beta}}_{\dir}:=\bm{Z}_{\dir}^{-1}\bm{u}_{\dir}
.

F.2
Interacted Estimators for the Total Effect

The full formula for the total effect variance is given by (
57
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{I_{T}}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{1}{J_{T}}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{1}{IJ}\left(\frac{I_{C}J_{C}}{I_{T}J_{T}}-1\right)\omega_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{BS}}\\
&+\frac{1}{I_{C}}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{1}{J_{C}}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{1}{IJ}\left(\frac{I_{T}J_{T}}{I_{C}J_{C}}-1\right)\omega_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{BS}}\\
&-\frac{1}{I}\xi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{B}}-\frac{1}{J}\xi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{1}{IJ}\xi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{BS}}.\end{split}

(106)

We can express the optimal adjustment
\bm{\beta}:=[\beta_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}\ \beta_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}]
as
\bm{\beta}=\bm{Z}^{-1}\bm{u}
, where
\bm{Z}
is a
2\times 2
block matrix with block entries

\displaystyle\bm{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}Z^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}Z^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{I_{C}J_{C}}{I_{T}J_{T}}\frac{1}{IJ}Z^{\textsf{BS}}

\displaystyle\bm{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{I}Z^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{1}{J}Z^{\textsf{S}}-\frac{1}{IJ}Z^{\textsf{BS}}

\displaystyle\bm{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}Z^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}Z^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{I_{T}J_{T}}{I_{C}J_{C}}\frac{1}{IJ}Z^{\textsf{BS}}

and
\bm{u}
is given by

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{I_{T}}u^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}-\frac{1}{I}(u^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}-u^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{J_{T}}u^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}-\frac{1}{J}(u^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}-u^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{IJ}\left(\frac{I_{C}J_{C}}{I_{T}J_{T}}-1\right)u^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}+\frac{1}{IJ}(u^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}-u^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}})

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{I_{C}}u^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}-\frac{1}{I}(u^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}-u^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{J_{C}}u^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}-\frac{1}{J}(u^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}-u^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{IJ}\left(\frac{I_{T}J_{T}}{I_{C}J_{C}}-1\right)u^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}+\frac{1}{IJ}(u^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}-u^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}).

This simplifies to

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}u^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}+\frac{1}{I}u^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}

\displaystyle+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}u^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}+\frac{1}{J}u^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{IJ}\left(\frac{I_{C}J_{C}}{I_{T}J_{T}}\right)u^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}-\frac{1}{IJ}u^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}u^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}+\frac{1}{I}u^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}

\displaystyle+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}u^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}+\frac{1}{J}u^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{IJ}\left(\frac{I_{T}J_{T}}{I_{C}J_{C}}\right)u^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}-\frac{1}{IJ}u^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}.

If we replace
y_{ij}(\gamma)
by the residuals
y_{ij}(\gamma)-X_{ij}^{\top}\beta_{\gamma}
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{u}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}(e)-\hat{\bm{u}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}\hat{Z}^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}\beta_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}+\frac{1}{I}\hat{Z}^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}\beta_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}

\displaystyle+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}\hat{Z}^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}\beta_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}+\frac{1}{J}\hat{Z}^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}\beta_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{IJ}\left(\frac{I_{C}J_{C}}{I_{T}J_{T}}\right)\hat{Z}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}\beta_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}-\frac{1}{IJ}\hat{Z}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}\beta_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{u}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}(e)-\hat{\bm{u}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}\hat{Z}^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}\beta_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}+\frac{1}{I}\hat{Z}^{\textsf{B}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}\beta_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}

\displaystyle+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}\hat{Z}^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}\beta_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}+\frac{1}{J}\hat{Z}^{\textsf{S}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}\beta_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{IJ}\left(\frac{I_{T}J_{T}}{I_{C}J_{C}}\right)\hat{Z}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}\beta_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}-\frac{1}{IJ}\hat{Z}^{\textsf{BS}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}\beta_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}.

We may write this as
\hat{\bm{u}}(e)-\hat{\bm{u}}=\hat{\bm{Z}}\bm{\beta}
where

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{Z}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{I_{C}}{I_{T}I}\hat{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{J_{C}}{J_{T}J}\hat{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{I_{C}J_{C}}{I_{T}J_{T}}\frac{1}{IJ}\hat{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{BS}}

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{Z}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{I}\hat{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{1}{J}\hat{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{S}}-\frac{1}{IJ}\hat{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{BS}}

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{Z}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{I}\hat{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{1}{J}\hat{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{S}}-\frac{1}{IJ}\hat{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{BS}}

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{Z}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{I_{T}}{I_{C}I}\hat{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{J_{T}}{J_{C}J}\hat{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{I_{T}J_{T}}{I_{C}J_{C}}\frac{1}{IJ}\hat{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{BS}}

It is instructive to compare this to least squares in each group. The OLS objective with constants is to minimize in
\beta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{I_{T}J_{T}}\sum_{(i,j)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}(y_{ij}^{(d)}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}})-X_{ij}^{(d)\top}\beta_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{I_{T}J_{T}}\sum_{(i,j)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}(y_{ij}^{(dcr)}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}})-X_{ij}^{(dcr)\top}\beta_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}})^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{I_{T}}\sum_{i\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}(\bar{y}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}})-\bar{X}_{i,\bullet}^{(d)\top}\beta_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}})^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{J_{T}}\sum_{j\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}(\bar{y}_{\bullet,j}^{(d)}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}})-\bar{X}_{\bullet,j}^{(d)\top}\beta_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}})^{2},

which suggests the population objective, up to constant factors,

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{I_{T}}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{1}{I_{T}}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{1}{I_{T}}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{tr}}}^{\textsf{BS}}\\
&+\frac{1}{I_{T}}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{B}}+\frac{1}{I_{T}}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{S}}+\frac{1}{I_{T}}\omega_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\operatorname{cc}}}^{\textsf{BS}}\end{split}

(107)

So compared to the optimal adjustment, the OLS upweights the decentered term and underweights the row and column means. Moreover, there is no interaction term.

F.3
Interacted Estimators for the Buyer Spillover Effect

For the buyer spillover estimator
\hat{\tau}_{\text{bs}}(\beta)
((
7
) with
c=[0,1,0,-1]
), the variance is given by

\displaystyle\Var(\tau_{\text{bs}}(\beta))
